<!-- thm-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:below}
Let ${\bf kU}$ denote the connective spectrum of (topological) complex K-theory.
If $p$ and  $\ell$ are distinct primes, then the maps 
$${\bf J}_\ell, \ {\bf J}_\ell \circ (\psi^p)_\ell:  ({\bf kU})_\ell \times_{{\bf K(\mathbb Z_\ell,0)}} {\bf K(\mathbb Z,0)} 
\quad \to \quad {\bf BG_\ell(S)}$$
are equal in the homotopy category of  connective $\Omega$-spectra.
<!-- end thm-1 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:below2}
Let ${\bf J}_\ell^o:  ({\bf BU})_\ell \ \to \ (\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_\ell$ denote the map 
of 1-connected $\Omega$-spectra induced by ${\bf J}_\ell$.
If $p$ and $\ell$ are distinct primes, then the following maps 
  $${\bf J}_\ell^o, \ {\bf J}_\ell^o \circ (\psi^p)_\ell:  ({\bf BU})_\ell 
\quad \to \quad (\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_\ell $$
are equal in the homotopy category of  connective $\Omega$-spectra.
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- cor-1: body_md -->
If $p$ and $\ell$ are distinct primes, the following maps of 1-connected spectra
$${\bf J}_{(\ell)}^o, \ {\bf J}_{(\ell)}^o \circ (\psi^p)_{(\ell)}:  ({\bf BU})_{(\ell)} 
\quad \to \quad (\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_{(\ell)} $$
are equal in the homotopy category of  connective $\Omega$-spectra.
<!-- end cor-1 -->
<!-- prop-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:Frob}
Let $V$ be an affine algebraic variety over $k$ with coordinate algebra $k[V]$,
a finitely generated commutative $k$-algebra.   Assume that $V$ is defined 
over some finite subfield $\mathbb F_q \subset k$ with $q = p^d$, so that $k[V] = F_q[V_{\mathbb F_q}] \otimes_{\mathbb F_q} k$
for some finitely generated commutative $\mathbb F_q$-algebra $\mathbb F_q[V_{\mathbb F_q})]$.    
\begin{enumerate}
\item
The $d$-th {\bf (geometric) Frobenius} $F^d: V \to V$ is map of $k$-varieties defined as
the base change to $k$ of the $q$-th power map
$V_{\mathbb F_q} \to V_{\mathbb F_q}$ given by sending $f \in \mathbb F_q[V_{\mathbb F_q}]$ to $f^q$.  
For $d = 1$, we use $F$ to denote $F^1$.
\item 
The  $d$-th {\bf (arithmetic) Frobenius} $\sigma^d: V \to V$ is the map of schemes (but
not of $k$-varieties) given
by $1 \otimes (-)^q: \mathbb F_q[V_{\mathbb F_q}] \otimes_{\mathbb F_q}  k \ \to \ k[V_{\mathbb F_q}] \otimes_{\mathbb F_q}  k$.
\item
The {\bf total $q$-th power map} \ $(-)^q = F^d \circ \sigma^d = \sigma^d \circ F^d: V \ \to \ V$ 
(also called the $d$-th {\bf (absolute) Frobenius})
is the map of schemes sending each  $f \in k[V]$ to $f^q$.  This map  induces the identity map 
on the (rigid) \`etale homotopy type and \`etale cohomology of $V$.
 \end{enumerate}
<!-- end prop-1 -->
<!-- defn-1: body_md -->
\label{defn:smash-alg}
Let $R$ denote either $\mathbb C$ (the complex numbers) or $k$ or the Witt vectors $W(k)$ of $k$ 
(a complete discrete valuation ring with residue field $k$ and field of fractions of characteristic 0).
Consider the simplicial mapping cone of the open embedding 
$\mathbb A_R^{n}{\text -}\{ 0 \} \hookrightarrow \mathbb A^n_R$,
\begin{equation}
\label{defn:S-alg}
S^{2n,alg}_R \ \equiv \ (\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits R) \cup_{(\mathbb A_R^n{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times 0)}  
(\mathbb A_R^n{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times \Delta[1])  \cup_{(\mathbb A_R^n{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times 1)}   \mathbb A_R^n,
\end{equation}
a simplicial $R$-scheme.  Since $GL_n$ acts naturally on the diagram
$$\ast \ \leftarrow \ \mathbb A^n{\text -}0  \stackrel{id \times 0}{\to} \ \mathbb A^n{\text -}0\times \Delta[1]
\ \stackrel{id \times 1}{\leftarrow } \  \mathbb A^n{\text -}0 \ \hookrightarrow \ \mathbb A^n,$$
there is a natural $GL_{n,R}$ action on $S^{2n,alg}_R$. 

The smash product 
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:define-wedge}
\wedge:  S^{2m,alg}_R \wedge S^{2n,alg}_R \quad \to \quad S^{2m+2n,alg}_R
\end{equation}
is the map of simplicial $R$-schemes which factors the composition of 
the natural map 
$$S^{2m,alg}_R \times S^{2n,alg}_R \ \to \ (\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits R) \cup_{\mathbb A_R^{m+n}{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times  L }
(\mathbb A_R^{m+n}{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times \Delta[1]^{\times 2})  \cup_{\mathbb A_R^{m+n}{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times (1,1) }   
\mathbb A_R^{m+n}$$
induced by the embedding $(\mathbb A_R^m{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times \Delta[1]) \times (\mathbb A_R^n{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times \Delta[1])
\ \hookrightarrow \ \mathbb A_R^{m+n}{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times \Delta[1]^{\times 2}$
and the map 
$$(\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits R) \cup_{\mathbb A_R^{m+n}{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times  L }
(\mathbb A_R^{m+n}{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times \Delta[1]^{\times 2})  \cup_{\mathbb A_R^{m+n}{\text-}\{ 0 \} \times (1,1) }   
\mathbb A_R^{m+n} \ \to S^{2m+2n,alg}_R$$
 induced by the simplicial map $\Delta[1]^{\times 2} \to \Delta[1]$
which sends $L \subset \Delta[1]^{\times 2} $ to the vertex 0 and sends the 
vertex $(1,1) \in \Delta[1]^{\times 2} $ to the vertex 1.

We verify that 
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:alg-equi}
\wedge: S^{2m,alg}_R \times S^{2n,alg}_R \ \to \ S^{2m+2n,alg}_R
\end{equation}
is $GL_{m,R} \times GL_{n,R}$-equivariant, by observing that $GL_{m,R} \times GL_{n,R}$
acts on the appropriate diagram.
<!-- end defn-1 -->
<!-- prop-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:Pn}
For any positive integer $d$, the
the maps on \'etale cohomology induced by the (geometric) Frobenius map $F$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:H-Pn}
H_{et}^{2s}(\mathbb P^n_k,\mathbb Z/\ell^d)  \ \stackrel{F^*}{\to} \ \ H_{et}^{2n}(\mathbb P^n_k,\mathbb Z/\ell^d), \quad \quad 
H_{et}^{2s}(S^{2n,alg}_k,\mathbb Z/\ell^d)  \ \stackrel{F^*}{\to} \ \ H_{et}^{2s}(S^{2n,alg}_k,\mathbb Z/\ell^d)
\end{equation} 
are identified with multiplication by $p^s$ on $\mathbb Z/\ell^d$. 
Moreover,
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:H*-S}
H_{et}^i(S^{2n,alg}_k,\mathbb Z/\ell^d) \simeq \mathbb Z/\ell^d, \ i = 0, 2n, \quad H_{et}^i(S^{2n,alg}_k,\mathbb Z/\ell^d) = 0, \ 
i \not=  0, 2n,
\end{equation}
and the smash product (\ref{eqn:define-wedge}) induces an isomorphism
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:et-iso}
H^*_{et}(S^{2m+2n,alg}_k,\mathbb Z/\ell^d) \quad \stackrel{\sim}{\to} \quad
H^*_{et}(S^{2m,alg}_k \wedge S^{2n,alg}_k,\mathbb Z/\ell^d).
\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-2 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
The assertion for $F^*$ on $H_{et}^{2s}(\mathbb P^n_k,\mathbb Z/\ell^d)$ follows from the functoriality of 
the cycle class map.  Using the ``geometric fibration" $\mathbb A^1_k{\text-}0 \to \mathbb A^n_k{\text-}0 \to \mathbb P_k^{n-1}$,
we conclude that the action of $F^*$ on $H_{et}^{2n-1}( \mathbb A^n_k{\text-}0,\mathbb Z/\ell^d)$ is identified with multiplication
by $p^n$ on $\mathbb Z/\ell^d$ and $H_{et}^{i}( \mathbb A^n_k{\text-}0,\mathbb Z/\ell^d) = 0, \ i \not= 0, 2n-1$.   

Computations involving the \'etale cohomology of the simplicial schemes $S^{2n,alg}_k$ are achieved by using the
spectral sequence $E_1^{s,t} = H^t_{et}(X_s,\mathbb Z/\ell^d) \Rightarrow H^{s+t}_{et}(X_\bullet,\mathbb Z/\ell^d)$ for the etale 
cohomology of a simplicial scheme $X_\bullet$ (see \cite[Prop 2.4]{F82}).  For the simplicial schemes occurring
in this proposition, the spectral sequences are readily compared with analogs occurring in topology.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- prop-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:map-cone}
We denote by $|S^{2n,alg}_\mathbb C|$ the total space of simplicial topological space $(S^{2n,alg}_\mathbb C)^{top}$ obtained
by applying the analytic topology functor to the simplicial scheme $S^{2n,alg}_\mathbb C$
over $\mathbb C$.  The Lie group $GL(n,\mathbb C)^{top}$ acts naturally on $|S^{2n,alg}_\mathbb C|$.  

Consider the continuous pointed map 
 \begin{equation}
 \label{eqn:tau-n}
 \gamma_n: |S^{2n,alg}_\mathbb C| \quad \to \quad (\mathbb C^n)^+
 \end{equation}
 given by sending $\ast \in |S^{2n,alg}_\mathbb C|$ to the base point $\infty \in (\mathbb C^n)^+$;
 sending $(z,t) \in \mathbb A^n{\text-}0 \times [0,\frac{1}{2}]$ to $\frac{z}{4t} \in (\mathbb C^n)^+$;
 sending $(z,t) \in \mathbb A^n{\text-}0 \times [\frac{1}{2},1]$ to $tz$, and sending 
 $\mathbb A^n$ (attached at $t=1$) via the inclusion in $(\mathbb C^n)^+$.
So defined, $\gamma_n$ is $\operatorname{GL}\nolimits_n^{top}$-equivariant as well as a pointed homotopy equivalence.

Then smash product determines the following commutative square whose maps are
$GL_m^{top} \times GL_n^{top}$-equivariant
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:GL-smash}
\xymatrix{
|S^{2m,alg}_\mathbb C| \times |S^{2n,alg}_\mathbb C| \ar[d]_{\gamma_m \times\gamma_n} \ar[r]^-\wedge & |S^{2m+2n,alg}_\mathbb C| \ar[d]^{\gamma_{m+n}} \\
(\mathbb C^m)^+ \times (\mathbb C^n)^+ \ar[r]^\wedge & (\mathbb C^{m+n})^+
.}
\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-3 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
We view $|S^{2n}_\mathbb C|$ is the mapping cone of the inclusion
$(A^n(\mathbb C){\text-}\{ 0 \})^{top} \hookrightarrow (A^n(\mathbb C))^{top}$.
We see by inspection that $\gamma_n$ is $GL_n^{top}$-equivariant.
 One way to see that $\gamma_n$ is a homotopy equivalence
 is by viewing $S^{2n}$ as homotopy equivalent to the the mapping cone of 
 $S^{2n-1} \hookrightarrow \mathbb R^{2n}$.

The commutativity of (\ref{eqn:GL-smash}) is verified by inspection.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- notation-1: body_md -->
\label{note:bar}
For a  closed subgroup scheme $\mathbb G_R$ of $GL_{N,R}$ for some 
$N$ (we shall take $R$ to be $\mathbb C$ or 
$W(k)$ or $k$), we denote by $B\mathbb G_R$ the simplicial $R$-scheme obtained by
applying the ``simplicial bar construction"; so defined, $B\mathbb G_R$ has $\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits R$ in simplicial 
degree 0 and $\mathbb G_R^{\times n}$ in simplicial degree $n > 0$; 
face maps $\mathbb G_R^{\times n} \to \mathbb G_R^{\times n-1}$ are determined by multiplications of adjacent 
copies of $\mathbb G_R$ as well as projections; degeneracy 
maps $\mathbb G_R^{\times n} \to \mathbb G_R^{\times n+1}$ involve projections 
and the identity map $\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits R \to \mathbb G_R$.   If $X_\bullet$ is a simplicial $R$-scheme
with $\mathbb G_R$ acting on the left on each $X_n$ and with each structure map of $X_n \to X_m$ a map of 
$\mathbb G_R$-schemes, then we define $B(\mathbb G_R,X_\bullet)$ to be the diagonal of the evident 
bisimplicial $R$-scheme:   $B(\mathbb G_R,X_\bullet)_0 = X_0$, \ $B(\mathbb G_R,X_\bullet)_n \ = \ 
\mathbb G_k^{\times n}\times_{\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits R} X_n$, and with face maps determined by multiplication 
for $\mathbb G_r$ except that the last face map entails the action $\mathbb G_R \times X_n \to X_{n-1}$.
More generally, if also given $Y_\bullet$  simplicial $R$-scheme
with $\mathbb G_R$ acting on the right on each $Y_n$, we consider $B(Y_\bullet,\mathbb G_R,X_\bullet)$,
 the diagonal of the evident tri-simplicial $R$-scheme.



In order to use a parallel construction for Lie groups $G^{top}$ acting continuously 
on a simplicial topological 
space $T$, we use the simplicial bar constructions to define $BG^{top}$ and $B(G^{top},T)$ as the
total spaces of the simplicial spaces $(BG^{top})_\bullet$ and $B(G^{top},T)_\bullet$; here 
$(BG^{top})_0$ is a point and $(BG^{top})_n$ the topological space $(G^{top})^{\times n}$ for $n > 0$;
$B(G^{top},T)_0$ is the space $T_0$ and $B(G^{top},T)_n$ is the space $(G^{top})^{\times n} \times T_n$; the face and
degeneracy maps are defined as above.

In what follows, we shall suppress the designation $(-)_\bullet$ for simplicial schemes and
simplicial spaces.
<!-- end notation-1 -->
<!-- prop-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:geom-fiber}
Consider the commutative square of simplicial $k$-varieties
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:tau-F}
\xymatrix{
B(GL_{n,k},S_k^{2n,alg}) \ar[d]_{\tau_n^{alg}} \ar[r]^F  & B(GL_{n,k},S_k^{2n,alg}) \ar[d]^{\tau_n^{alg}} \\
BGL_{n,k} \ar[r]_F & BGL_{n,k}
}
\end{equation}
whose horizontal maps are the (geometric) Frobenius map and whose vertical maps are the natural projections.
Then the restriction of \ $F: B(GL_{,k},S_k^{2n,alg}) \to B(GL_{n,k},S_k^{2n,alg})$ above
the base point $\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits k \to BGL_{n,k}$ is the Frobenius map $F: S_k^{2n,alg} \to S_k^{2n,alg}$.
<!-- end prop-4 -->
<!-- defn-2: body_md -->
\label{defn:ell-complete}
We employ the $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completion functor of \cite[X.4.9,X.4.10]{Bo-Kan}
$$(\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty)(-): (s.sets_*) \ \quad \to \quad (s.sets_*)$$
(taking values in Kan complexes)
which has the important property that $(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty(f): (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty(X) \to 
(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty(Y)$ is a homotopy equivalence whenever the map $f:X \to Y$
of simplicial sets is a mod-$\ell$ equivalence (i.e., whenever $f$
 induces an isomorphism $H_*(X,\mathbb Z/\ell) \ \stackrel{\sim}{\to} \ H_*(Y,\mathbb Z/\ell)$).
Another useful property of $(\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty)(-)$ is that the natural map $(\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty)(X\times Y) 
\to (\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty)(X) \times (\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty)(Y)$ is a homotopy equivalence with
a natural left inverse \cite[I.7.2]{Bo-Kan}.

To simplify notation, we shall often abbreviate the name of the functor 
$(\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty)(-)$ by setting
$$(-)_\ell \ \equiv \ (\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty)(-): (s.sets_*) \quad \to \quad (s.sets_*).$$
<!-- end defn-2 -->
<!-- defn-3: body_md -->
\label{defn:pro-type}
As discussed in \cite[\S 4, \S 8]{F82}, the ($\check{C}$ech) \'etale topological type functor on the 
category of locally noetherian schemes
$$(-)_{ret}: (\text{geometrically pointed schemes}) \quad \to \quad (pro{\text-}s.sets_*)$$
is defined by setting 
$$X_{ret}: (\mathcal C(X,r{{\text - }}et))^{op} \ \to \ (s.sets_*), \quad (U \to X) \ \mapsto \ \pi_0(N_X(U)),$$ 
sending a rigid \'etale cover $U \to X$ to 
the simplicial set of connected components of its $\check{C}$ech nerve.
<!-- end defn-3 -->
<!-- defn-4: body_md -->
\label{defn:wedge}
For a geometrically pointed, connected simplicial scheme $X_\bullet$ and a prime $\ell$, we define the $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completed
rigid \'etale topological type functor 
$$(-)^\wedge: (s.schemes_*)  \quad \to \quad (s.sets_*)$$
by sending the geometrically pointed simplicial scheme $X_\bullet$ to
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:hat}
(X_\bullet)^\wedge \quad \equiv \quad (diag\{ n \mapsto \underset{\longleftarrow}{holim}((X_n)_{ret})_\ell) \})_\ell,
\end{equation}
where $diag\{ - \}: (bis.sets_*) \to (s.sets_*)$ denotes the diagonal functor sending a bi-simplicial set to its diagonal.  
(See \cite[App B]{Bo-F}.)

If $X_\bullet$ is not connected, then we require a geometric pointing of each connected component  of $X_\bullet$
and then take the disjoint union of  the result of applying $(-)^\wedge$ to each component.
<!-- end defn-4 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
\label{rem:explain}

In the above definition, we apply $(-)_\ell$ after taking the diagonal of a bisimplicial set
because the diagonal of a simplicial object of Kan complexes is not necessarily a 
Kan complex.   

For the schemes considered in this paper, the natural map
$(X_n)_{ret} \to ((X_n)_{ret})_\ell$ induces an isomorphism on $\mathbb Z/\ell$-cohomology
(i.e., $(X_n)_{ret}$ is $\ell$-good) and  $\pi_1((X_n)_{ret})$ acts trivially on $H^*((X_n)_{ret},\mathbb Z/\ell)
= H^*_{et}(X_n,\mathbb Z/\ell)$.
Because $\overline X_n$ has bounded cardinality and the homotopy groups of are finitely generated 
$\mathbb Z_\ell$-modules, this implies that $\underset{\longleftarrow}{holim}((X_n)_{ret})_\ell)$ is also 
$\ell$-good. 

Consequently, the $\mathbb Z/\ell$-cohomology of $diag\{ n \mapsto \underset{\longleftarrow}{holim}((X_n)_{ret})_\ell) \}$
equals the \'etale cohomology $H^*_{et}(X_n,\mathbb Z/\ell)$.  Thus, applying $(-)_\ell$ after taking the 
diagonal of bisimplicial set does not change the $\mathbb Z/\ell$-cohomology and yields a Kan complex.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- thm-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:holim} 
Let $X_\mathbb C$ be a pointed, connected simplicial scheme which is of finite type over $\mathbb C$
in each simplicial degree.  Then there
exist homotopy equivalences
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:Phi_X} 
Sin(X(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell \
\quad \stackrel{\rho_{U\mapsto X}}{\leftarrow} \quad \underset{\longleftarrow}{holim} (Sin((N_X(U)_\bullet(\mathbb C))^{top})_\ell
\quad \stackrel{\rho_{top\mapsto \pi_0}}{\to} \quad (X_\mathbb C)^\wedge
\end{equation}
natural with respect to pointed maps of simplicial schemes over $\mathbb C$.
<!-- end thm-3 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
The map $\rho_{U\mapsto X}$ is determined level-wise maps for each $n \geq 0$
 by applying $Sin(|N_{X_n}(-)^{top}|)$ to rigid  \'etale coverings $U \to X_n$,
whereas the map $\rho_{top\mapsto \pi_0}$ is determined level-wise by the ``inverse system" of maps \\
$Sin(N_{X_n}(U)^{top}) \to \pi_0(N_{X_n}(U)^{top})$.
The proof that these maps  are homotopy equivalences can be found in \cite[Thm 8.4, Cor 8.5]{F82}.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- prop-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:comparison} 
Equip $W(k)$ with an embedding into $\mathbb C$ and consider a connected simplicial scheme $X_{W(k)}$ 
over $W(k)$ with a section $\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits W(k) \to (X_{W(k)})_0$ such $X_{W(k)}$ is of  finite type over $W(k)$
in each simplicial degree.  Assume that $X_{W(k)}$
satisfies the \'etale cohomological base change isomorphisms of (\ref{eqn:base-change}).
Then base change determines pointed homotopy equivalences
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:natural} 
(X_\mathbb C)^\wedge  \quad \stackrel{\rho_{W \mapsto \mathbb C}}{\leftarrow} \quad
 (X_{W(k)})^\wedge  \quad \stackrel{\rho_{W \mapsto k}}{\rightarrow}  \quad (X_k)^\wedge.
 \end{equation}
 
 These equivalences are natural with respect to maps $X_{W(k)} \to Y_{W(k)}$ 
defined over $\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits W(k)$.
<!-- end prop-5 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
\label{rem:alt-def}
Proposition \ref{prop:comparison} provides motivation/justification for our definition of
$(-)^\wedge$.  In \cite{F82}, \'etale coverings (and hypercoverings) of simplicial schemes
were considered, rather than considering ``level-wise" \'etale coverings.  This works well if 
one considers pro-objects in the homotopy category of pointed simplicial sets but the
rigidification process seems questionable for such coverings.

Another approach is due to G. Quick in \cite{Quick} and \cite{Quick2} whose ``\'etale topological type"
of a simplicial scheme is a simplicial object in a suitable homotopy category for 
pro-spaces.  We find this approach elegant, and an interested reader might find
Quick's formulation to be preferable to that given in Definition \ref{defn:wedge} though
perhaps requiring additional machinery in the context of the homotopy category of pro-spaces.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- ex-1: body_md -->
\label{ex:S-alg}
$S^{n,alg}_{W(k)}$ satisfies the hypotheses of Proposition \ref{prop:comparison}.
This can be verified by considering base changes at each level of the simplicial schemes.  See the
proof of Proposition \ref{prop:Pn}.
<!-- end ex-1 -->
<!-- cor-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:product}
Consider pointed, connected
simplicial schemes $X_{W(k)}, \ Y_{W(k)}$ over $\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits W(k)$ of finite type in each dimension which satisfy the
base change equivalences of (\ref{eqn:natural}).  Assume that $X \times Y$ satisfies
the condition that the natural map 
$$H^*(X(\mathbb C)^{top},\mathbb Z/\ell) \otimes H^*(Y(\mathbb C)^{top},\mathbb Z/\ell)  \quad \to \quad H^*(X(\mathbb C)^{top} \times Y(\mathbb C)^{top},\mathbb Z/\ell)$$
is an isomorphism.  Then  the natural map 
$$(X_R \times  Y_R)^\wedge \ \to \ (X_R)^\wedge \times (Y_R)^\wedge$$
is a pointed homotopy equivalence whenever $R$ equals $k, \ \mathbb C,$ or $W(k)$.
<!-- end cor-2 -->
<!-- prop-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:hom-fiber}
There is a natural homotopy equivalence from $ (S_k^{2n,alg})^\wedge$ to the 
homotopy fiber of the map obtained by applying $(-)^\wedge$ to the projection map $\tau_{n,k}^{alg}$:
$$(\tau_{n,k})^\wedge: (B(GL_{n,k},S_k^{2n,alg}))^\wedge \ \to \ (BGL_{n,k})^\wedge.$$ 

The commutative squares of  (\ref{eqn:tau-F}) of simplicial $k$-varieties determines
$$F^\wedge: (\tau_{n,k})^\wedge \quad \to \ \quad F^{\wedge*}((\tau_{n,k})^\wedge)$$
 whose map on homotopy fibers is homotopic to 
$F^\wedge: (S_k^{2n,alg})^\wedge \ \to \ (S_k^{2n,alg})^\wedge$
which has degree $p^n$.
<!-- end prop-6 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
By Proposition \ref{prop:geom-fiber}, $S_k^{2n,alg}$ is the geometric fibre of $\tau_{n,k}$.
By \cite[Thm 10.7]{F82}, there is a natural $\mathbb Z/\ell$-equivalence from 
$(S_k^{2n,alg})^\wedge$ (the ``homotopy
type of the geometric fiber") to the homotopy fiber of $(\tau_{n,k})^\wedge$ (the 
``homotopy-theoretic fiber").
Using Example \ref{ex:S-alg} and Proposition \ref{prop:comparison}, we conclude that the second 
assertion follows from the naturality of the comparison of geometric and homotopy-theoretic
fibers.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- defn-5: body_md -->
\label{defn:assoc-spec}
Let $T$ be the finite simplicial set with two non-degenerate simplices and with
$|T| = S^1$.  Let $T^n$ denote the $n$-th smash power of $T$.  For any 
$\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$, we extend $\underline\mathcal B$ so that it is a functor from 
finite pointed simplicial sets to simplical sets.  

Following \cite[Prop 1.4]{Segal} and \cite[Thm 4.4]{Bo-F}, we define the spectrum
$$|| \underline\mathcal B|| \quad \equiv \quad \{ \underline\mathcal B(T^n), \ T \wedge \underline\mathcal B(T^n) \to \underline\mathcal B(T^{n+1})\}$$
associated to $\underline\mathcal B$.
<!-- end defn-5 -->
<!-- ex-2: body_md -->
\label{ex:cN}
We denote by $\underline \mathcal N: \mathcal F \to (sets_*)$  the functor sending ${\bf n}$ for $n > 0$ to the discrete
pointed simplicial set $\mathbb N^{\times n}$ (the pointed set of $n$-tuples of non-negative integers)
and sending $\alpha: {\bf n} \to {\bf m}$ in $\mathcal F$ to the pointed set map
$\mathbb N^{\times n} \to \mathbb N^{\times m}$ which sends the $n$-tuple $I=(i_1,\dots,i_n)$ to the $m$-tuple
$\alpha(I) = (j_1,\dots,j_m)$ where $j_t = \sum_{\alpha(s)= t} i_s$ and the sum is the
monoid structure of $\mathbb N$.

The spectrum $||\underline\mathcal N ||$ has $\pi_0$ equal to $\mathbb N$.  The associated $\Omega$-spectrum
 $||\underline\mathcal N ||^+$ (see (\ref{eqn:equiv})) can be identified with ${\bf K(\mathbb Z,0)}$.
<!-- end ex-2 -->
<!-- thm-4: body_md -->
\cite[\S 5]{Bo-F}
\label{thm:summary}
Denote by $vs\mathcal F[s.sets_*]$  the full subcategory of $\mathcal F[s.sets_*]$
whose objects are very special $\mathcal F$-spaces.
There is a functor 
 \ $T: \mathcal F[s.sets_*] \to vs\mathcal F[s.sets_*]$ \ together with a 
natural transformation $\eta: id \to T$ which induces a map on homotopy categories
fitting in the following commutative square
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:equiv}
\xymatrix{
Ho(\mathcal F[s.sets_*]) \ar[d]_T \ar[r] ^{||-||} & Ho(c.spectra) \ar[d]^+ \\
Ho(vs\mathcal F[s.sets_*])  \ar[r] & Ho(c.\Omega\text{-}spectra)
.}
\end{equation}
\begin{enumerate}
\item
The lower horizontal map of (\ref{eqn:equiv}) is an equivalence of categories, where
$Ho(c.\Omega{\text -}spectra)$ is the ``usual" stable homotopy category of connective spectra.
\item
For any $\mathcal F$-space $: \underline\mathcal B$, $\eta: \underline\mathcal B \to T(\underline\mathcal B)$
is a stable weak equivalence in the sense of \cite{Bo-F}.
\item
If $\underline\mathcal B$ is a special $\mathcal F$-space, then $\eta: ||\underline\mathcal B|| \to ||T(\underline\mathcal B)||$ is a ``homotopy-theoretic group
completion" on $0$-spaces  $||\underline\mathcal B||_0 \to ||T(\underline\mathcal B)||_0$ and determines homotopy equivalences
$||\underline\mathcal B||_i \to ||T(\underline\mathcal B)||_i$ for $i > 0$.
\item
If $\underline\mathcal B$ is a very special $\mathcal F$-space, then $\eta: ||\underline\mathcal B|| \to ||T(\underline\mathcal B)||$ 
is a homotopy equivalence of $\Omega$-spectra.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-4 -->
<!-- construct-1: body_md -->
\label{construct:homotopy}
Denote by $\mathcal F{\text -}spec$ the full subcategory of $\mathcal F[s.sets_*]$ of special 
$\mathcal F$-spaces.  Then $\mathcal F{\text -}spec$ inherits the structure of a Quillen
model category from that of $\mathcal F[s.sets_*]$ such that
$$Ho(\mathcal F{\text -}spec) \quad  \to \quad Ho(\mathcal F[s.sets_*])$$
 is fully faithful.  Namely, one verifies 
that any fibrant replacement and any cofibrant replacement of an object of 
$\mathcal F{\text -}spec$ is a map in $\mathcal F{\text -}spec$.

Moreover, the slice category $\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N$ inherits the structure of a Quillen
model category from that of $\mathcal F{\text -}spec$ such that
$$Ho(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N) \quad  \to \quad Ho(\mathcal F[s.sets_*])$$
is also fully faithful.

Denote by $(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*$ the full subacategory of $\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N$
whose objects 
$$f: (\underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal N, \ s: \underline\mathcal N \to \underline\mathcal B;\  f\circ s = id)$$ 
are special $\mathcal F$-spaces over $\underline\mathcal N$  equipped with a section
with the additional property the pre-image
$\underline\mathcal B_I$ of any $I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$ is pointed, connected.  Then 
$(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*$ inherits the structure of a Quillen
model category from that of $\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N$ such that
$$Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*) \quad  \to \quad Ho(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)$$
is fully faithful.  The justification for this is that fibrant and cofibrant replacements
in $\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N$ of objects in $(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*$ can
be achieved using functorial base-point preserving constructions 
level-by-level (see, for example, \cite{Bo-F}), and the base point of the connected 
space $\underline\mathcal B_I$ for 
$(\underline\mathcal B,f,s)$ for each $I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$ is given by $s(I)$.
<!-- end construct-1 -->
<!-- cor-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:homotopy-cat}
Two maps $\phi, \psi: {\underline \mathcal B} \to {\underline \mathcal B}^\prime$ in $(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*$
are equal in \\
$Hom_{\mathcal F[s.sets_*]}(\underline\mathcal B, \underline\mathcal B^\prime)$ if and only if they are equal in 
$Hom_{(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*}(\underline\mathcal B, \underline\mathcal B^\prime)$.   

Moreover, if $\phi, \psi$ are equal in $Hom_{(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*}(\underline\mathcal B, \underline\mathcal B^\prime)$,
then they are homotopic in the stable homotopy category of 
connective spectra.
<!-- end cor-3 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
The constructions in this paper involve objects and maps in $(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*$.
The additional structure for an $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal N$ to be an object of $(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*$
enables internal structure maps to be base-point preserving, needed when one takes smash products.

We refer to an object of $(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*$ as a ``pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$",
leaving the structure map $f: \underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal N$ and the section $s: \underline\mathcal N \to \underline\mathcal B$ implicit.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- ex-3: body_md -->
\cite[Ex 8.1]{F80}, \cite{Segal}
\label{ex:BGL}
There exists a special $\mathcal F$-object over $\underline\mathcal N$ of pointed topological spaces 
$${\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top}: \mathcal F \quad \to \quad (\text{spaces}_*)$$
with ${\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top}({\bf 1})\ = \ \coprod_{n\geq 0} BGL_n(\mathbb C)^{top}$ 
and sum pairing given by block sum of matrices.  Here,
$BGL_n(\mathbb C)^{top}$ is the total topological space associated to the simplicial bar construction 
applied to the complex Lie group $GL_n(\mathbb C)^{top}$ which we view as the nerve of the 
topological category with one object $\mathbb C^n$ and whose mapping space is $GL_n(\mathbb C)^{top}$.
For $n > 1$ and $I \in \underline\mathcal N$, we identify 
${\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top}({\bf n})_I$ with
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:id-GL}
{\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top}({\bf n})_I \quad \simeq \quad
\prod_{i=1}^n  BGL_{n_i}(\mathbb C)^{top} \times \prod_{S\subset {\bf n}} E(\Sigma_{|S|},\Sigma_{|S|}).
\end{equation}
This is  the nerve of the topological category $\mathcal C(GL)({\bf n})$ an object of which is indexed by pairs
$(I = (i_1,\ldots,i_n) \in \mathcal F({\bf n}), \times_{S\subset {\bf n}} \sigma_S \in \Sigma_{|S|})$ where $S\subset {\bf n}$ 
is the set of subsets of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$  of size $|S| \geq 2$.  Maps of $\mathcal C(GL)({\bf n})$
consist of isomorphisms of objects, where $Iso((I \in \mathcal F({\bf n}), \times_{S\subset {\bf n}} \sigma_S \in \Sigma_{|S|})$
can be identified with $\prod_{j=1}^n GL_{i_j}(\mathbb C)^{top} \times \prod_{S\subset {\bf n}} \Sigma_{|S|}$.
Sending ${\bf n}$ to $\mathcal C(GL)({\bf n})$ determines a well-defined
functor from $\mathcal F$ to (topological categories), where elements $\mu_S \in \Sigma_{|S|}$ are used to 
provide functorial maps between partial sums indexed by $S \subset {\bf n}$. 
Taking the nerve of these categories, one obtains the ``special" $\mathcal F$-object 
${\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top}: \mathcal F \to \ (\text{spaces}_*)$ over $\underline\mathcal N$.

Moreover, there is a map of special $\mathcal F$-objects over $\underline\mathcal N$ of
topological spaces
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:tau-top-spec}
\tau^{top}_{\mathbb C^+}: {\underline \mathcal B}(GL(\mathbb C),\mathbb C^+)^{top} \to {\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top}: \ \mathcal F \quad \to \quad (\text{spaces}_*)
\end{equation}
with the property that 
$${\underline \mathcal B}(GL(\mathbb C),\mathbb C^+)^{top}({\bf 1})\ = \ \coprod_{n\geq 0} B(GL_n(\mathbb C),(\mathbb C^n)^+)^{top} \ \to$$
$$\coprod_{n\geq 0} BGL_n(\mathbb C)^{top} \ = \ {\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top}({\bf 1})$$
is the projection.  

The construction of ${\underline \mathcal B}(GL(\mathbb C),\mathbb C^+)^{top} $ is given by taking the nerves of 
 the topological categories $\widetilde{\mathcal C(GL)}({\bf n})$, an object of which is indexed by pairs
$$(z \in \wedge_{j=1}^n (\mathbb C^{i_j})^+,\ \times_{S\subset {\bf n}} \sigma_S \in \Sigma_{|S|}).$$
Again, the indexing set $S\subset {\bf n}$ is the set of subsets of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$  of size $|S| \geq 2$.
Maps of $\widetilde{\mathcal C(GL)({\bf n})}$ consist of isomorphisms of objects which respect sums; these 
can be identified with $\prod_{j=1}^n GL_{i_j}(\mathbb C)^{top}$.
<!-- end ex-3 -->
<!-- ex-4: body_md -->
\label{ex:tau}
Proposition \ref{prop:map-cone} enables us to replace $(\mathbb C^n)^+$ by $|S^{2n,alg}_\mathbb C|$
in the construction of $\tau^{top}_{\mathbb C^+}$, thereby obtaining
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:tau-S2alg-top}
\tau_{|S^{2,alg}_\mathbb C|}^{top}: {\underline \mathcal B}(GL(\mathbb C),|S^{2,alg}_\mathbb C|)^{top} \to {\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top}: 
\ \mathcal F \quad \to \quad (\text{spaces}_*)
\end{equation}
<!-- end ex-4 -->
<!-- ex-5: body_md -->
\label{ex:G(|X|)}
Let $X$ denote a pointed, connected, locally finite simplicial set with realization $|X|$.  Denote by 
$G(|X|)^{top}$ the function space with the compact open topology of pointed self-equivalences
of $|X|$.    Composition gives $G(|X|)^{top}$ the structure of an $H$-space.
Moreover, smash product $X^m \times X^n \to X^{m+n}$ determines  a continuous
smash product $|X^m|\times |X^n| \to |X^{m+n}|$ leading to the smash product 
$G(|X^m|)^{top} \times G(|X^n|)^{top} \to G(|X^{m+n}|)^{top}$ of self-equivalences and a continuous action of 
$G(|X^m|)^{top} \times |X^m| \to |X^m|$ commuting with this smash product.

There exists a special $\mathcal F$-object over $\underline\mathcal N$ of pointed topological spaces 
$${\underline \mathcal B}G(|X|)^{top}: \mathcal F \quad \to \quad (\text{spaces}_*)$$
with ${\underline \mathcal B}G(|X|)^{top}({\bf 1})\ = \ \coprod_{n\geq 0} BG(|X^n|)^{top}$ 
and sum pairing given by smash products.   As in the previous example,
$BG(|X^n|)^{top}$ is the total topological space associated to the simplicial bar construction 
applied to the $H$-space $G(|X^n|)^{top}$.  For $n > 1$ and $I = (i_1,\ldots,i_n)\in \underline\mathcal N$, we identify 
${\underline \mathcal B}G(|X|)^{top}({\bf n})_I$ with
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:id-GL-I}
{\underline \mathcal B} G(|X|)^{top}({\bf n})_I \quad \simeq \quad
\prod_{j=1}^n  BG(|X^{i_j}|)^{top} \times \prod_{S\subset {\bf n}} E(\Sigma_{|S|},\Sigma_{|S|}).
\end{equation}

As in the previous example, there there is a map of special $\mathcal F$-objects over $\underline\mathcal N$ of
topological spaces
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:tau-top}
\pi^{top}_{|X|}: {\underline \mathcal B}(G(|X|),|X))^{top} \to {\underline \mathcal B}G(|X|)^{top}: \ \mathcal F \quad \to \quad (\text{spaces}_*)
\end{equation}
with the property that 
$${\underline \mathcal B}(G(|X|),|X|)^{top}({\bf 1})\ = \ \coprod_{n\geq 0} B(G(|X^n)|,|X^n|)^{top} \ \to$$
$$\coprod_{n\geq 0} BG(|X^n|)^{top}\ = \ {\underline \mathcal B}G(|X|)^{top}({\bf 1})$$
is the projection.
<!-- end ex-5 -->
<!-- defn-6: body_md -->
\label{defn:G(X)}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set with geometric realization $|X|$.  Recall 
that $|-|$ is left adjoint to the singular functor $Sin(-): (spaces_*) \to (s.sets_*)$.
We consider the 
function complex $\underline{Hom}_*(X,Sin(|X|))$ whose $t$-simplices are simplicial maps
$X \times \Delta[t] \to Sin(|X|)$ subject to the condition that $x_0 \times \Delta[t]$ maps to 
$x_0 \subset Sin_0(|X|)$ where $x_0 \in X_0$ is the base point.  The adjunction $| - | \circ Sin(-) \to id$ equips 
$\underline{Hom}_*(X,Sin(|X|))$ with a simplicial monoid structure associated with composition
of functions and defines the natural action
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:nat-act}
\underline{Hom}_*(X,Sin(|X|)) \times Sin(|X|) \quad \to \quad Sin(|X|).
\end{equation}

We denote by $G(X) \ \subset \ \underline{Hom}_*(X,Sin(|X|))$ 
the (simplicial) submonoid consisting of components whose images in 
the discrete monoid $\pi_0 (\underline{Hom}_*(X,Sin(|X|)))$ are invertible, and we
denote by  $G^o(X) \subset G(X)$ the distinguished component containing 
the canonical map $X \to Sin(|X|)$.  

If the action of each $\sigma \in \Sigma_n$ on smash 
factors of $X^n$ is weakly homotopy equivalent to the identity of $X^n$, 
then we say that $\Sigma_n \subset G^o(X^n)$.

The adjunction equivalence \ $\underline{Hom}_*(X,Sin(|X|)) \ \stackrel{\sim}{\to} \ Sin(\underline{Hom}^{top}_*(|X|,|X|)$
implies that $G(X)$ is $G(X)$ is a Kan complex.  If $X$ is locally finite, then $G(X) \ \simeq Sin(G(|X|)^{top})$.
<!-- end defn-6 -->
<!-- ex-6: body_md -->
\label{ex:sphere-spectrum}
If $|X|$ has the homotopy type of the (pointed) $n$-sphere $S^n$, 
then $G(X)$ is homotopy equivalent to $Sin(\Omega_{\pm 1}^n(S^n))$
and $G^0(X)$ is homotopy equivalent to $Sin(\Omega_1^n(S^n))$.  Thus,
$\pi_i(G(X)) = \pi_{i+n}(S^n)$ for $i > 0$ and $\pi_0(G(X)) = \mathbb Z/2$.  
See, for example, a discussion by J.F. Adams in \cite[\S2]{Adams}.
<!-- end ex-6 -->
<!-- ex-7: body_md -->
\label{ex:G(X)-act}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set.
For $I = (i_1,\ldots,i_n) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$, denote by $X^I$ the product $\prod_{j=1}^n X^{i_j}$, 
where $X^{i_j}$ is the smash product of $i_j$ copies of $X$.  We
consider the simplicial monoid $G(X^I) \ \subset \ \underline{Hom}_*(X^I,Sin(|X^I|))$ with product 
given by composition (together with the adjunction map $|-| \circ Sin \to id$).
\begin{enumerate}
\item
Let $\alpha: {\bf n} \to {\bf m}$ be a non-decreasing map in $\mathcal F$, $I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$, 
and $J = \alpha(I) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf m})$.  Then $\alpha$ determines maps involving smash products
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:smash-prod}
X^I \ \stackrel{\alpha}{\to} \  X^J, \quad G(X^I) \ \stackrel{\alpha}{\to} \  G(X^J).
\end{equation}
\item
For $\alpha: {\bf n} \to {\bf m}$ non-decreasing, the natural actions of the form (\ref{eqn:nat-act})
fit into commutative squares 
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:compat}
\xymatrix{
G(X^I) \times X^I \ar[r] \ar[d]_\alpha &  Sin(|X^I |) \ar[d]^\alpha \\
G(X^J) \times X^J  \ar[r] &  Sin(|X^J |).
}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}

The compatible pairings of (\ref{eqn:compat}) determine the map of special $\mathcal F$-spaces over $\underline\mathcal N$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:pi-X}
\pi_X:  \underline\mathcal B (G(X),Sin(|X|))  \quad \to \quad \underline\mathcal B G(X)
\end{equation}
whose value on ${\bf 1}$ is the projection $\coprod_{i \geq 0} B(G(X^i),Sin(|X^i|)) \ \to \ \coprod_{i \geq 0} BG(X^i).$
As in previous examples, we can identify the restriction of $\pi_X$ above $I \in \mathcal N({\bf n})$ as the projection 
$$
\prod_{i=1}^n  BG(X^n),Sin(|X^n)) \times \prod_{S\subset {\bf n}} E(\Sigma_{|S|},\Sigma_{|S|}) \ \to \ 
\prod_{i=1}^n  BG(X^n) \times \prod_{S\subset {\bf n}} E(\Sigma_{|S|},\Sigma_{|S|}).
$$


If $\Sigma_n \subset G^o(X^n)$ for all $n > 0$, then we define the ``oriented analogue" 
 $\underline \mathcal B G^o(X) \hookrightarrow \ Sin(\underline\mathcal B G(|X|)^{top,o})$
by simply replacing $G(X^I)$ by $G^o(X^I)$  in the construction of $\underline\mathcal B G(X)$.
<!-- end ex-7 -->
<!-- prop-7: body_md -->
\label{prop:close-relate}
Let $X$ denote a pointed, connected, locally finite simplicial set with realization $|X|$. 
Then there is a commutative square of pointed-connected special $\mathbb F$-spaces over $\underline\mathcal N$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:compat-relate}
\xymatrix{
\underline\mathcal B (G(X),Sin(|X|))  \ar[r]  \ar[d]_{\pi_X} &  \ar[d]^{\pi^{top}_{|X|} } Sin(\underline\mathcal B (G(|X|),|X|)^{top}) \\
 \underline\mathcal B G(X) \ar[r]_{adj} & Sin(\underline\mathcal B G(|X|)^{top})
}
\end{equation}
whose horizontal maps arise using the canonical maps $G(X^I) \to Sin(G(|X^I|))$.
For every $I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n}), n > 0$, the restriction of (\ref{eqn:compat-relate}) above $I$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:compat-I}
\xymatrix{
\underline\mathcal B (G(X),Sin(|X|))_I  \ar[r]  \ar[d]_{\pi_X} &  \ar[d]^{\pi^{top}_{X} } Sin(\underline\mathcal B (G(|X|),|X|)^{top})_I \\
 \underline\mathcal B G(X)_I  \ar[r]_{adj}  & Sin(\underline\mathcal B G(|X|))_I
}
\end{equation}
is a cartesian square whose vertical maps are fibrations with fibers homotopy
equivalent to $Sin(|X^I |)$ and whose horizontal maps are weak equivalences.
<!-- end prop-7 -->
<!-- defn-7: body_md -->
\label{defn:X-fibration}
Consider  a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$ and a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$
over $\underline\mathcal N$.  An $X$-fibration over $\underline\mathcal B$ is a pair of maps 
\ $(f: \underline\mathcal E \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B, \ s_f: \underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal E)$ \ of special $\mathcal F$-spaces over $\underline \mathcal N$
with $f \circ s_f = id$ satisfying the following conditions:
\begin{enumerate}
\item
For each $n > 0$ and $I =(i_1,\ldots,i_n) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$, $f_I: \underline\mathcal E_I\to \underline\mathcal B_I $ is a fibration with fibers which
are pointed homotopy equivalent to $Sin(|X^I|)$.
\item
For any non-decreasing $\alpha: {\bf n} \to {\bf  m} \in \mathcal F$,  $I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$,  $J = \alpha(I) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf m})$,
and $\Delta[d] \to \underline\mathcal B_I$, the restriction of $\alpha$ above $\Delta[d]$, 
$$\alpha_{I,\Delta[d]}: \underline\mathcal E_I \times_{\underline\mathcal B_I} \Delta[d] \ \to \ \underline\mathcal E_J \times_{\underline\mathcal B_J} \Delta[d],$$
is homotopic to $(\wedge_{\alpha,I})\times id: Sin(|X^I |)\times \Delta[d] \quad \to \quad Sin(|X^J|) \times \Delta[d].$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end defn-7 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{rem:Reedy}
Definition \ref{defn:X-fibration} is a simplified formulation of the definition of an $X$-fibration given in
\cite{F80} and corrected in \cite{B-K}.  We have replaced the somewhat cumbersome
condition that an $X$-fibration $f:\underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ be ``properly sectioned" 
by the condition that $f$ is equipped with a left inverse.  This enables compatible 
base points on fibers of $f$.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- defn-8: body_md -->
\label{defn:X-map}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set.
Consider a map $\phi: \underline\mathcal B \ \to \ \underline \mathcal B^\prime$ of pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-spaces over
$\underline\mathcal N$, and $X$-fibrations $(f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline \mathcal B, \ s_f: \underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal E)$ \ 
$(f^\prime: \underline\mathcal E ^\prime\to \underline \mathcal B^\prime, \ s_{f^\prime}: \underline\mathcal B^\prime \to \underline\mathcal E^\prime)$.
A map $(\tilde \phi,\phi): (f,s_f) \ \to \ (f^\prime,s_{f^\prime})$  of $X$-fibrations over $\phi$ is  
a commutative square  in $(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*$.
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:F-map}
\xymatrix{
\underline\mathcal E \ar[r]^{\tilde \phi}  \ar[d]_f  &  \underline\mathcal E^\prime \ar[d]^{f^\prime} \\
\underline\mathcal B \ar[r]_{\phi }& \underline\mathcal B^\prime
}
\end{equation}
compatible with sections for $f$ and $f^\prime$
with the property that the induced map ${\underline\mathcal E}_I  \ \to \ {\underline\mathcal E^\prime}_I \times_{\underline\mathcal B_I^\prime} \underline\mathcal B_I$
is a fiber homtopy equivalence over $\underline\mathcal B_I$ for all $I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$.
<!-- end defn-8 -->
<!-- ex-8: body_md -->
\label{ex:X-fib}
We give a few examples of $X$-fibrations.
\begin{enumerate}
\item
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set.
The map of $\mathcal F$-spaces 
$$\pi_X: \underline\mathcal B (G(X),Sin(|X|)) \quad \to \quad \underline\mathcal B G(X)$$
 of Example \ref{ex:G(X)-act}
is a $X$-fibration over $\underline\mathcal B G(X)$.
\item
If $X$ is a pointed, connected simplicial set such that $\Sigma_n \subset G^o(X^n)$ for all $n > 0$, 
then $\pi^o_X$ restricts to the $X$-fibration
$$\pi^o_X: \underline\mathcal B (G^o(X),Sin(|X|)) \quad \to \quad \underline\mathcal B G^o(X).$$
\item
Applying $Sin(-)$ to the map $\tau^{top}_{\mathbb C^+}:{\underline \mathcal B}(GL(\mathbb C),\mathbb C^+)^{top} \to {\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top}$ of (\ref{eqn:tau-top})
determines the $X$-fibration
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:tau-C+}
\tau_{\mathbb C^+}: Sin({\underline \mathcal B}(GL(\mathbb C),\mathbb C^+)^{top}) \quad \to \quad  Sin({\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top})
\end{equation}
 for some pointed simplicial set $X$ with $|X| \simeq \mathbb C^+$.
\item
Similarly, if $T_n(\mathbb C) \subset GL_n(\mathbb C)$ denotes the diagonal torus, we obtain
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:tau-T-C+}
\tau_{T,\mathbb C^+}: Sin({\underline \mathcal B} (T(\mathbb C),\mathbb C^+)^{top}) \quad \to \quad  Sin({\underline \mathcal B}T(\mathbb C)^{top})
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end ex-8 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:iota!-construct}
Consider a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$ and a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$,
and an $X$-fibration  $(f:\underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B, \ s_f: \underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal E$).  Let $\iota: \underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal B^\prime$ be a
trivial cofibration of pointed, connected special $\mathcal F$-spaces over $\underline\mathcal N$ and 
factor $\iota \circ f$ as $\iota_!(f) \circ j: \underline\mathcal E  \ \to \ \underline\mathcal E^! \to \underline\mathcal B^\prime$ with $j$ a 
trivial cofibration and  $\iota_!(f)$ a fibration.
Then $\iota_!(f): \underline\mathcal E^! \to \underline\mathcal B^\prime$ is an $X$-fibration over $\underline\mathcal B^\prime$ (with section 
$s_{f^!}: \underline\mathcal B^\prime \to \underline\mathcal E^!$ obtained by extending $\iota \circ s_f$).
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Consider 
$\iota^*(\iota_!(f)):  \underline\mathcal E^! \times_{\underline\mathcal B^\prime} \underline\mathcal B \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B$ and 
some $n > 0$ and $I \in \mathcal F({\bf n})$.  Observe that
 $\underline\mathcal E^! \times_{\underline\mathcal B^\prime} \underline\mathcal B \ \to \ \underline\mathcal E^!$ is a weak equivalence,
the pull-back of the weak equivalence $\iota$ along $(\iota_!(f))_I$.  (This uses the fact that 
the category of $(s.sets_*)$ is right proper.  See \cite{nLab}.)  Thus, the map of fibrations
$f_I \to (\iota^*(\iota_!(f)))_I$ is a map of fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B_I$ whose map on total spaces is 
a weak equivalence.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- defn-9: body_md -->
\label{defn:Xfib}
Consider a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$ and a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$.
We define an equivalence relation on $X$-fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B$ by setting  $f \ \sim \ f^\prime$
if and only there is a chain of weak equivalences of pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-spaces over $\underline\mathcal N$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:self-chain}
\underline\mathcal B \ \stackrel{p}{\twoheadleftarrow} \quad \underline\mathcal B_1 \quad \stackrel{\phi}{\to} \quad \underline\mathcal B_2  
\quad \stackrel{j}{\hookleftarrow}  \ \quad \underline\mathcal B
\end{equation}
and a  map of $X$-fibrations $p^*(\underline\mathcal E) \to j_!(\underline\mathcal E^\prime)$ covering $\phi: \underline\mathcal B_1 \to \underline\mathcal B_2$
(with sections)
$$p^*(\underline\mathcal E) \quad \to \quad \iota_!(\underline\mathcal E^\prime),$$
where $p$ is a cofibrant replacement of $\underline\mathcal B$, $j$ is a fibrant replacement of $\underline\mathcal B^\prime$,  and the 
image of (\ref{eqn:self-chain}) in $Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(\underline\mathcal B,\underline\mathcal B)$ is the identity.

We denote by $X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B)$ the set of equivalence classes of $X$-fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B$,
which we view as ``fiber homotopy equivalence classes" of $X$-fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B$.
<!-- end defn-9 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{rem:restate}
Two $X$-fibrations $f , \ f^\prime$ over $\underline\mathcal B$ are equivalent if and only if 
there is a chain of weak equivalences in $(\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:chain-equiv}
\underline\mathcal B \ \stackrel{\phi_1}{\to} \ \underline\mathcal B_1 \ \stackrel{\phi_2}{\leftarrow} \ \underline\mathcal B_2 \to \cdots \to  \underline\mathcal B_{2n-1}
\stackrel{\phi_{2n}}{\leftarrow} \underline\mathcal B
\end{equation}
whose image in $Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(\underline\mathcal B,\underline\mathcal B)$ is the identity and which is 
covered by a chain of maps of $X$-fibrations $f \to f^1 \leftarrow f^2 \to \cdots \to f^{2n} \leftarrow f^\prime$.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:iota!}
Consider a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$ and a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$.
If $\iota: \underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal B^\prime$ is a trivial cofibration, then sending an $X$-fibration $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ to the
$X$-fibration $\iota_!*(f): \underline\mathcal E^! \to \underline\mathcal B^\prime$ determines a bijection 
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:iota}
\iota_!: X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B) \quad \to \quad X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B^\prime).
\end{equation} 

If $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ is an $X$-fibration over $\underline \mathcal B$,
there is a natural map $f  \ \to \ \iota^*(\iota_!(f))$ of $X$-fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B$,
and if $f^\prime: \underline\mathcal E^\prime \to \underline\mathcal B^\prime$ is an $X$-fibration over $\underline \mathcal B^\prime$,
there is a  natural map $\iota_!(\iota^*(f^\prime)) \ \to \ f^\prime$ of $X$-fibrations 
over $\underline\mathcal B^\prime$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
To show that $\iota$ is well defined on equivalence classes of $X$-fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B$, 
it suffices to observe that $\iota$ applied to (\ref{eqn:chain-equiv}) determines a commutative
diagram
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:i!-map}
\xymatrix{
\underline\mathcal B \ar[d]_\iota  & \ar[l]_{\stackrel{p}{\twoheadleftarrow}} \ar[d] \underline\mathcal B_1 \ar[r]^\phi \ &  \underline\mathcal B_2  \ar[d] &
 \underline\mathcal B  \ar[d]^\iota \ar[l]_{\stackrel{j}{\hookleftarrow}} \\
\underline\mathcal B^\prime  & \ar[l]^{\stackrel{p^\prime}{\twoheadleftarrow}}  \underline\mathcal B^\prime_1 \ar[r]_{\phi^\prime}  &  \underline\mathcal B^\prime_2   & \underline\mathcal B^\prime  
\ar[l]^{\stackrel{j^\prime}{\hookleftarrow}}
}
\end{equation}
where the left square is chosen to be a pull-back square, the right square is a pushout square, and $\phi^\prime$ is 
chosen to make the middle square commute. 

Consider $\iota^*(\iota_!(f)):  \underline\mathcal E^! \times_{\underline\mathcal B^\prime} \underline\mathcal B \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B$ and 
some $n > 0$ and $I \in \mathcal F({\bf n})$.  Observe that
 $\underline\mathcal E^! \times_{\underline\mathcal B^\prime} \underline\mathcal B \ \to \ \underline\mathcal E^!$ is a weak equivalence,
the pull-back of the weak equivalence $\iota$ along $(\iota_!(f))_I$.  (This uses the fact that 
the category of $(s.sets_*)$ is right proper.  See \cite{nLab}.)  Thus, the map of fibrations
$f_I \to (\iota^*(\iota_!(f)))_I$ is a map of fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B_I$ whose map on total spaces is 
a weak equivalence.  Using the long exact sequence for homotopy groups of a fibration,
we conclude that $f_I \to (\iota^*(\iota_!(f)))_I$ induces homotopy equivalences on fibers
so that $f \to \iota^*(\iota_!(f))$ is a map of $X$-fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B$.  
 
 On the other hand, given an $X$-fibration $f^\prime: \underline\mathcal E^\prime \to \underline\mathcal B^\prime$ over $\underline\mathcal B^\prime$,
 the fibration of $\iota_!(\iota^*(f^\prime)): (\iota^*(\underline\mathcal E^\prime)^! \to \underline\mathcal B^\prime$ factors through
 $f^\prime$ with  $\iota_!(\iota^*(f^\prime)) \to f^\prime$ a map of $X$-fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B^\prime$.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:p*}
Consider a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$, a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$, 
and a trivial fibration $p: \underline\mathcal B^{\prime\prime} \to \underline\mathcal B$. Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:p*}
p^*:  X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B) \quad \to \quad X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B^{\prime\prime})
\end{equation}
is well defined bijection.

Moreover, if $f^{\prime\prime}  : \underline\mathcal E^{\prime\prime}  \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B^{\prime\prime} $ is an $X$-fibration 
over $\underline\mathcal B^{\prime\prime} $, then the composition $p\circ f^{\prime\prime} : \underline\mathcal E^{\prime\prime} \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B$
is an $X$-fibration over $\underline\mathcal B$ with the property that the natural map $p^*(p\circ f^{\prime\prime}) \to f^{\prime\prime}$
is a map of $X$-fibration over $\underline\mathcal B^{\prime\prime}$.  Furthermore, if $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ is an $X$-fibration
over $\underline\mathcal B$, then $p \circ p^*(f): \underline\mathcal E\times_{\underline\mathcal B} \underline\mathcal B^{\prime\prime} \to \underline\mathcal B^{\prime\prime} \to \underline\mathcal B$
naturally maps to $f$ as a map of $X$-fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B$.

Consequently, $p^*$ of (\ref{eqn:p*}) is bijective.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- prop-8: body_md -->
\label{prop:functor}
 Consider a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$.
 Sending a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$ to $X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B)$
 and using the bijections of Lemmas \ref{lem:iota!}.and \ref{lem:p*}, 
 we obtain the functor
 $$X{\text-}fib(-): Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)^{op} \quad \to \quad (sets). $$
 
 Consequently, sending $\phi: \underline\mathcal B \dashrightarrow \underline\mathcal B G(X)$ to $\phi^*(\pi_X) \in X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B)$
 determines a well defined natural transformation of functors
 \begin{equation}
 \label{eqn:upper-X}
 (-)^*(\pi_X): Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(-,\underline\mathcal B G(X)) \quad \to \quad X{\text-}fib(-).
 \end{equation}
<!-- end prop-8 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
If $\phi: \underline\mathcal B \dashrightarrow \underline\mathcal B_1$ is a map in $Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)$, 
let $p: \underline\mathcal B^{\prime\prime} \twoheadrightarrow \underline\mathcal B$ be a cofibrant replacement and let 
$\iota:\underline\mathcal B_1 \hookrightarrow \underline\mathcal B_1^\prime$ be a fibrant replacement.  
Represent $\phi$ by a chain of the form 
$\underline\mathcal B \ \stackrel{p}{\leftarrow} \ \underline\mathcal B^{\prime\prime} \ \stackrel{f}{\to} \ \underline\mathcal B_1^\prime \ \stackrel{\iota}{\leftarrow} \ \underline\mathcal B_1.$
We define
$$X{\text-}fib(\phi) \ \equiv \ (p \circ) \circ  f^* \circ  \iota_!: \ X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B_1) \quad \to \quad  X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B),$$
which can be viewed as $(p^*)^{-1} \circ f^* \circ (\iota^*)^{-1}$ by Lemmas \ref{lem:iota!} and \ref{lem:p*}.  

Granted that
two choices of $f: \underline\mathcal B^{\prime\prime} \to \underline\mathcal B^\prime_1$ representing $\phi$ are related by a homotopy from
a cyclinder object for $\underline\mathcal B^{\prime\prime} $ to $\underline\mathcal B^\prime_1$, the pull-back $f^*$ is independent of this 
choice.  To verify that the composition is independent of choices of cofibrant and fibrant replacements is a 
straight-forward argument involving the comparison of two such choice by ``dominating" them with a third.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- defn-10: body_md -->
\label{defn:principal-simplex}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set.
Consider  an $X$-fibration $f: \underline\mathcal E \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B$ (with section $s:\underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal E$ implicit)
for some pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$.
 For $I = (i_,\ldots,i_n) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$, we say that a map $\phi: X^I \times \Delta[d]
 \to Sin(|\underline\mathcal E_I |)$ is a principal $d$-simplex of $f_I$ provided that
 \begin{enumerate}
 \item
 The composition $f_I \circ \phi: X^I \times \Delta[d] \to Sin(|\underline\mathcal E_I |) \to Sin(|\underline\mathcal B_I |)$ factors though the
 projection, $X^I \times \Delta[d] \to \Delta[d] \to Sin(|\underline\mathcal B_I |)$ whose associated map 
 $X^I \times \Delta[d] \ \to \ Sin(|\underline\mathcal E_I |) \times_{Sin(|\underline\mathcal B_I |)} \Delta[d]$ restricted to each fiber
 over $\Delta[d]$  is weakly homotopic to the canonical pointed map $X^I \to Sin(|X^I|)$.
 \item
 Consider a non-decreasing map $\alpha: {\bf n} \to {\bf m}$ in $\mathcal F$ and
 set $J= (j_1,\ldots,j_m) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf m}), \ j_s = \sum_{\alpha(i) = s} i_s$.
 Then $\phi$ induces $\phi^\alpha: X^J \times \Delta[d] \to Sin(|\underline\mathcal E_{J} |)$ fitting in the commutative square
 \begin{equation}
\xymatrix{
X^I \times \Delta[d] \ar[d]_{\alpha} \ar[r]^-\phi &  Sin(|\underline\mathcal E_I|) \ar[d]^{\alpha} \\
X^J \times \Delta[d] \ar[r]^-{\phi^\alpha} & Sin(|\underline\mathcal E_J|)
}
\end{equation}
such that $\phi^\alpha$ restricted to each fiber over $\Delta[d] \to Sin(|\underline\mathcal B_I |)$ is weakly
homotopy equivalent to the canonical map $X^J\to Sin(|X^J|)$.
 
For $I = (i_,\ldots,i_n) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$, we define 
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:prin(f)}
P(f_I) \quad \subset \quad \underline{Hom}(X^I,Sin(|\mathcal E_I |)) 
\end{equation}
to be the subcomplex of  principal simplices of $f_I$ .  
By construction, there is a natural map of simplicial sets $P(f)_I \to Sin(|\underline\mathcal B_I|)$.
 \end{enumerate}
<!-- end defn-10 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:prin-X}
Consider a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$ and a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$.
Let $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ be an $X$-fibration (with section $s:\underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal E$ implicit).  Consider the  right action
of $G(X^I)$ on $P(f_I)$ for some $I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:prin-act}
P(f_I) \times G(X^I) \quad \to \quad P(f_I)
\end{equation}
which sends the pair
$(\phi: X^I \times \Delta[d] \to Sin(|\underline\mathcal E_I |),\ \theta: X^I \times \Delta[d] \to Sin(| X^I |))$ to 
the composition
$$X^I \times \Delta[d] \stackrel{\theta,pr_2}{\to} Sin(| X^I |) \times \Delta[d] \stackrel{Sin\circ |\phi|}{\to} 
Sin(|(Sin(|\mathcal E_I |)|))   \to Sin(|\mathcal E_I |).$$


The action of (\ref{eqn:prin-act}) fits in commutative squares involving compositions 
and smash products for any non-decreasing  $\alpha: {\bf n} \to {\bf m} \in \mathcal F$,
$I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$, and $J = \alpha(I)$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:prin-compat}
\xymatrix{
P(f_{I}) \times G(X^I) \ar[r] \ar[d]_\alpha & P(f_I) \ar[d]^\alpha \\
P(f_{J}) \times G(X^J) \ar[r]  & P(f_J). 
}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- prop-9: body_md -->
\label{prop:2-sided}
Consider a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$, a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$,
and an $X$-fibration $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ (with section $s:\underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal E$ implicit).
The diagrams (\ref{eqn:prin-compat}) for all $\underline n$ and $I \in \underline\mathcal N(\underline n)$ 
determine a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B (P(f),G(X))$ and (using the 2-sided bar construction)
the $X$-fibration $\pi_{P(f),X}: \underline\mathcal B (P(f),G(X),X) \to \underline\mathcal B (P(f),G(X))$.  

Moreover, $\pi_{P(f),X}$ fits in the following commutative diagram of $\mathcal F$-spaces
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:2-sided-func}
\xymatrix{
\underline\mathcal E \ar[d]_f \ar[r] &Sin(|\underline\mathcal E|) \ar[d]^{Sin(|f|)} & \ar[l]_-{\tilde p_f} \underline\mathcal B (P(f),G(X),X)
 \ar[d]^{\pi_{P(f),X}} \ar[r]^-{\tilde q_f} &   \underline\mathcal B (G(X),X) \ar[d]_{\pi_X} &  \\
\underline\mathcal B \ar[r]_\iota & Sin(|\underline\mathcal B|)  & \ar[l]^-{p_f} \underline\mathcal B (P(f),G(X)) \ar[r]_{q_f} &  \underline\mathcal B G(X) 
}
\end{equation}
whose squares are maps of $X$-fibrations and whose left and middle horizontal maps are weak equivalences.
<!-- end prop-9 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
For any $I = (i_1,\ldots,i_n) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$, Example \ref{ex:G(X)-act} and Lemma \ref{lem:prin-X} determine
commutative diagram
 of pointed simplicial sets
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:2-sided}
\xymatrix{
Sin(| \underline\mathcal E |)_I \ar[d]^{f_I}  & \ar[l]^-{\tilde p_{f,I}} \underline\mathcal B(P(f),G(X),X) _I
 \ar[d]^{\pi_{P(f),X,I}} \ar[r]^-{\tilde q_{f,I}}  &   
 B(G(X),X)_I \ar[d]_{\pi_{X,I}} &  \\
Sin(|\underline\mathcal B|)_I  & \ar[l]^-{p_{f,I}} B(P(f),G(X))_I \ar[r]_{q_{f,I}} &  BG(X)_I.
}
\end{equation}
The simplicial set $\underline\mathcal B(P(f),G(X),X) _I $ given by the 2-sided bar construction 
has set of degree $t$ simplices equal to
$$P(f_I)_t \times G(X^I)^{\times t} \times Sin(|X^I|)_t \times (\prod_{S\subset {\bf n}} \Sigma_{|S|})^{t+1}.$$

 The right horizontal maps of (\ref{eqn:2-sided}) are projections (sending $P(f_I)$ to the base point),  
so that the right squares of (\ref{eqn:2-sided}) are cartesian.  The lower left map, $p_{f,I}$, 
is given by projection to $P(f)_I$ followed by $P(f)_I  \to Sin(|\underline\mathcal B_I|)$.
The upper left map $\tilde p_{f,I}$ utilizes the right action of $G(X^I)$ on
$P(f_I)$ and the left action of $G(X^I)$ on $Sin(|X^I|)$.  
Then $\tilde p_{f,I}$ sends a $d$-simplex of this simplicial set represented (with $\Delta[d]$ omitted)
as \ $(\phi:X^I \to Sin(|\mathcal E_I|),\ (g_1,\ldots,g_t) \in G(X^I)^{\times t}, 
\ y \in Sin|(X^I|), \ \underline \sigma \in (\prod_{S\subset {\bf n}} \sigma_{|S|})^{t+1}$  
to the $d$-simplex of $Sin(| \underline\mathcal E |)_I$ represented as 
$(\phi\circ g_1)(g_t \circ y)$, where the actions $(-)\circ g_1$ and $g_t \circ (-y)$ are given
by Example \ref{ex:G(X)-act} and Lemma \ref{lem:prin-X}, ``twisted"  by $\underline \sigma$.
So defined, the left squares of (\ref{eqn:2-sided}) are commutative and homotopy cartesian. 

The assertion that the diagrams (\ref{eqn:2-sided}) determine the commutative diagram of $\mathcal F$-spaces
(\ref{eqn:2-sided-func}) is the assertion that maps of (\ref{eqn:2-sided}) are functorial
with respect to maps $\alpha: {\bf n} \to {\bf m} \in \mathcal F$ (sending $I\in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$ to 
$J= \alpha(I) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf m})$).  This is essentially implicit in the construction of these $\mathcal F$-spaces
which entails the compatibility of $G(X^I)$-actions with smash products.  
Since the squares of (\ref{eqn:2-sided}) are cartesian, the squares of  (\ref{eqn:2-sided-func})
are maps of $X$-fibrations.

For any simplex $\Delta[d] \to \underline\mathcal B_I$, there is a $G(X^I)$-equivariant weak equivalence \\
$G(X^I) \times \Delta[d]  \to P(f_I)\times_{\underline\mathcal B_I} \Delta[d]$. 
This implies that the lower left map of (\ref{eqn:2-sided-func}) has contractible fibers 
and thus is a a weak equivalence.  The 
left square of (\ref{eqn:2-sided}) is an equivalence of $X$-fibrations in view of
 the fact that for any simplical set $Y$ the 
natural map $Y \to Sin(|Y|)$ is a weak equivalence.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- thm-5: body_md -->
(cf. \cite[Thm 6.1]{F80})
\label{thm:X-universal}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set.
 The  natural transformation of Proposition \ref{prop:functor}
 \begin{equation}
(-)^*(\pi_X):  Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(-, \underline\mathcal B G(X)) \quad \to \quad X{\text-}fib(-) 
\end{equation}
is an isomorphism of functors with inverse
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:nat-trans}
\rho_X:  X{\text-}fib(-) \quad \to \quad Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*})(-, \underline\mathcal B G(X))
\end{equation}
sending the equivalence class  in $X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B)$ of an $X$-fibration $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$  to the homotopy class 
of the composition 
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:rho}
\rho_X(f) \quad \equiv \quad q_f \circ p_f^{-1} \circ \iota: \underline\mathcal B \dashrightarrow \underline\mathcal B G(X)
\end{equation}
 as in (\ref{eqn:2-sided-func}).

If $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ is an $X$-fibration, then a representative of $\rho_X(f): \underline\mathcal B \dasharrow \underline\mathcal B G(X)$
is called a classifying map for $f$.
<!-- end thm-5 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
The diagram (\ref{eqn:2-sided-func}) displays an arbitrary $X$-fibration $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ as equivalent to the pull-back along 
$\rho_X(f): \underline\mathcal B \dashrightarrow \underline\mathcal B G(X)$ of $\pi_X$.   Thus, 
$(-)^*(\pi_X):  Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(\underline\mathcal B, \underline\mathcal B G(X)) \quad \to \quad X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B)$ \ is 
surjective.

If $f \equiv \phi^*(\pi_x)$ is equivalent to $f^\prime \equiv \phi^{\prime*}(\pi_X)$, 
then this equivalence gives a chain of diagrams of $X$-fibrations covering the identity
of $\underline\mathcal B$ in $Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(\underline\mathcal B,\underline\mathcal B)$.  Focusing on the base of these chains 
of $X$-fibrations, we see that \\
$\phi = \phi^\prime \in Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(\underline\mathcal B, \underline\mathcal B G(X))$.
In other words, \\
$(-)^*(\pi_X):  Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(\underline\mathcal B, \underline\mathcal B G(X)) \quad \to \quad X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B)$
\ is also injective.

Observe that if $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ is an $X$-fibration whose equivalence class is represented by
$\rho_X(f): \underline\mathcal B \dashrightarrow \underline\mathcal B G(X)$, then $f$ is equivalent to $(\rho_X(f))^*(\pi_X) \ = \
(\rho_X \circ (-)^*(\pi_X))(f)$ (by the definitions of these constructions).
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- defn-11: body_md -->
\label{defn:smash-ell-good}
We recall that a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$ is called $\ell$-good if the 
canonical $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completion map $X \to X_\ell$ is a mod-$\ell$-equivalence
(i.e., induces an isomorphism in mod-$\ell$ homology).  
We say that a pointed simplicial set $X$ is ``smash $\ell$-good" if the $m$-fold smash 
product $X^m$ of $X$ is $\ell$-good for all $m > 0$.

Assume that $X$ is smash $\ell$-good.  For $I = (i_1,\ldots i_n) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$, we  denote by
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:action1}
G_\ell(X^I) \ \quad \hookrightarrow \quad \underline{Hom}_*(X^I,(X^I)_\ell)
\end{equation}
the (simplicial) function complex consisting of those $t$-simplices $\sigma: X^I \times \Delta[t] \to (X^I)_\ell$
which are mod-$\ell$ equivalences.   We denote by $G_\ell^o(X^I) \subset G_\ell(X^I)$ the subcomplex
of those $t$-simplices $\sigma: X^I \times \Delta[t] \ \to (X^I)_\ell$ with the property that
$\sigma_\ell: (X^I \times \Delta[t])_\ell \ \to ((X^I)_\ell)_\ell \to (X^I)_\ell$ is homotopic to the identity.
<!-- end defn-11 -->
<!-- prop-10: body_md -->
\label{prop:G-ell(X)-act}
Let $X$ be a pointed simplicial set which is smash $\ell$-good.
Consider some $I = (i_1,\ldots,i_n) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item 
$G_\ell(X^I)$ is a simplicial monoid and $G_\ell^o(X^I) \ \subset \ G_\ell(X^I)$ 
is a simplicial sub-monoid, where the product structure is induced by composition of functions.
\item 
There is a natural action $G_\ell(X^I) \times X^I \ \to \  (X^I)_\ell$ 
determining an inclusion of simplicial monoids
$$G_\ell(X^I) \quad \hookrightarrow \quad \underline{Hom}_*(X^I,(X^I)_\ell).$$
\item 
If $\alpha: {\bf n} \to {\bf m}$ non-decreasing,  $I \in \mathcal F({n})$ and $J = (j_1,\ldots j_m)$ with $j_s = \sum_{\alpha(i) = s}i$,
the action of (2) fits in a commutative square involving compositions and smash products
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:smash}
\xymatrix{
G_\ell(X^I) \times X^I \ar[r] \ar[d]_\alpha & (X^I)_\ell \ar[d]^\alpha \\
G_\ell(X^J) \times X^J \ar[r] & (X^J)_\ell .
}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end prop-10 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
The ``composition product" $G_\ell(X^I) \times G_\ell(X^I) \ \to \ G_\ell(X^I)$
is defined by sending $\gamma_1, \gamma_2: \Delta[d] \times X^I \to (X^I)_\ell$ to the 
composition
\begin{equation}
\Delta[d] \times X^I \ \stackrel{pr_1\times \gamma_2}{\to} \ \Delta[d]\times (X^I)_\ell \to 
(\Delta[d]\times X^I)_\ell \ \stackrel{(\gamma_1)_\ell}{\to} \ ((X^I)_\ell)_\ell \ \stackrel{\eta}{\to} \ (X^I)_\ell \ ,
\end{equation}
where $\eta: (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty \circ (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty \to (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty$ is part of the natural 
triple structure (see, for example, \cite{MacL}). 

The map $\underline{Hom}_*(X^I,X^I_\ell) \ \to \ \underline{Hom}_*((X^I)_\ell,(X^I)_\ell)$
commutes with composition thanks to the ``idempotence" of $\eta$.  This readily 
implies the commutativity of (\ref{eqn:smash}).
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- defn-12: body_md -->
\label{defn:ell-fibration}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set which is smash $\ell$-good.
A $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration over a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space 
\\ ${\underline \mathcal B}: \mathcal F \to (s.sets_*)$
is a  pair of maps \ ($f: \underline\mathcal E \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B, s: \underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal E)$ \ of special $\mathcal F$-spaces 
over $\underline \mathcal N$ with $s \circ f = id$  satisfying the following conditions:
\begin{enumerate}
\item
For each $n > 0$ and $I =(i_1,\ldots,i_n) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$, $f_I: \underline\mathcal E_I\to \underline\mathcal B_I $ is a fibration with fibers which
are pointed homotopy equivalent to $(X^I)_\ell$.
\item
For any non-decreasing $\alpha: {\bf n} \to {\bf  m} \in \mathcal F$,  $I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$,  $J = \alpha(I) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf m})$,
and $\Delta[d] \to \underline\mathcal B_I$, the restriction of $\alpha$ above $\Delta[d]$, 
$$\alpha_{I,\Delta[d]}: \underline\mathcal E_I \times_{\underline\mathcal B_I} \Delta[d] \ \to \ \underline\mathcal E_J \times_{\underline\mathcal B_J} \Delta[d],$$
is homotopic to $(\wedge_{\alpha,I})\times id: (X^I)_\ell \times \Delta[d] \quad \to \quad (X^J)_\ell \times \Delta[d].$
\end{enumerate}

A map of $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibrations
$(\tilde \phi,\phi)$ from $(f:\underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B, \ s:\underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal E)$  to
$(f^\prime:\underline\mathcal E^\prime \to \underline\mathcal B^\prime, s^\prime: \underline\mathcal B^\prime \to \underline\mathcal E^\prime)$ 
is a commutative square of $\mathcal F$-spaces which restricts for each $I \in \mathcal F({\bf n})$
to a fiber homotopy equivalence of the form (\ref{eqn:F-map}).
<!-- end defn-12 -->
<!-- ex-9: body_md -->
\label{ex:BGX-X-ell}
Assume that $X$ is a smash $\ell$-good, pointed, connected simplicial set.
In a manner parallel to the construction of the $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B G(X)$, 
the actions \ $G_\ell(X^I) \times X^I \ \to \ (X^I)_\ell$
for all $I \in \mathcal N({\bf n})$ determine the pointed special $\mathcal F$-space over $\underline\mathcal N$
$$\underline \mathcal B G_\ell(X): \mathcal F \quad \to \quad (s.sets_*)$$
with
$$ \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X)({\bf 1}) \ = \ \coprod_{n\geq 0} B G_\ell(X^n) \ = \ \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X)({\bf 1}), \quad
{\underline \mathcal B}G_\ell(X)_I \ = \ 
BG_\ell(X^I) \times \prod_{S\subset {\bf n}} E(\Sigma_{|S|},\Sigma_{|S|}).$$


Similarly, in parallel to the $X$-fibration $\pi_X: \underline\mathcal B(G(X),X) \to \underline\mathcal B G(X)$ of 
Example \ref{ex:G(X)-act}, these actions 
determine the $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:pi-univ-ell}
\pi_{X,\ell}: \underline \mathcal B (G_\ell(X),X_\ell) \quad \to \quad \underline \mathcal B G_\ell(X)
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:explicit-X-ell}
{\underline \mathcal B}G_\ell(X)({\bf n})_I \quad \simeq \quad
BG_\ell(X^I) \times \prod_{S\subset {\bf n}} E(\Sigma_{|S|},\Sigma_{|S|}).
\end{equation}


If $\Sigma_n \subset G(X^n)$ for all $n > 0$, then we similarly define
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:pi-univ-ell-o}
\pi_{X,\ell}^o: \underline \mathcal B (G_\ell^o(X),X_\ell) \quad \to \quad \underline \mathcal B G_\ell^o(X).
\end{equation}
<!-- end ex-9 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
\label{rem:contrast}
We emphasize that the construction of $\pi_{X_\ell}: \underline \mathcal B (G_\ell(X),X_\ell) \ \to \ \underline \mathcal B G_\ell(X)$
differs from that of Example \ref{ex:G(X)-act} with $X$ replaced by $X_\ell$.
One difference is that  $(X^m)_\ell$ is not the $m$-fold smash 
product of $X_\ell$; another is that $G_\ell(X) \ \not= \ G(X_\ell)$.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- prop-11: body_md -->
\label{prop:X-ell-cases}
Let $X$ be a smash $\ell$-good, pointed, connected simplicial set and consider a
pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline \mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item
The fiber-wise $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completion $(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(f)$ of an $X$-fibration 
$f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ is a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration over $\underline\mathcal B$.
\item
If $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ is a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration over $\underline\mathcal B$, then the
fiber-wise $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completion $(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(f)$ is also a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration over $\underline\mathcal B$
and the canonical map $f \to (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(f)$ is a fiber homotopy equivalence
of $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B$.
\item
If $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ is an $X$-fibration  and if 
$\pi_1(\underline\mathcal B_I)$ acts nilpotently on $H^*(X^I,\mathbb Z/\ell)$ for all $n > 0$, all $I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$,
then $(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty(f): \underline\mathcal E_\ell \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B_\ell$
is a  $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration over $\underline\mathcal B_\ell$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end prop-11 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
The first assertion follows from the functoriality of the fiber-wise $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completion
functor sending conditions of Definition \ref{defn:X-fibration} to conditions 
of Definition \ref{defn:ell-fibration}.  To prove assertion (2), observe that
if the fibers of $f_I$  are homotopy equivalent to $(X^I)_\ell$, then the fibers of
$(\mathbb Z/\ell)^\bullet(f_I)$ are homotopy equivalent to $((X^I)_\ell)_\ell$ which is naturally 
homotopy equivalent to $(X^I)_\ell$.  Namely, using the natural commutative square 
\begin{equation}
\xymatrix{
X^I \ar[d] \ar[r] &  (X^I)_\ell \ar[d] \\
(X_\ell)^I \ar[r] & ((X_\ell)^I)_\ell
}
\end{equation}
and the fact that the $(\mathbb Z/\ell)$-completion of
$(X^I)_\ell$ of the $\ell$-good simplicial set $X^I$ is also $\ell$-good, we conclude that
if $X$ is smash $\ell$-good then so is $X_\ell$.

Granted the hypotheses of assertion (3), the  Bousfield--Kan``mod-$\mathbb Z/\ell$ fibre lemma" 
(see \cite[II.5.1]{Bo-Kan}) implies  that the natural map from the $(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty$-completion of 
the fiber of $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B_I$ to the fiber of  $(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty(f)$ is a homotopy
equivalence; in other words, the fiber of $(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty(f)$ is homotopy equivalent 
to $(X^I)_\ell$.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
The $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completed $S^2$-fibrations occurring in (\ref{eqn:base-change-ss}) provide
further examples of $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completed $X$-fibrations which are not included among the 
examples of Proposition \ref{prop:X-ell-cases}.
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- defn-13: body_md -->
\label{defn:X-ell-fib}
Consider a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$  which is smash $\ell$-good and a 
pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space 
$\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$.
We define an equivalence relation on $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B$  by setting  $f \ \sim \ f^\prime$
if and only if there is a chain of weak equivalences of pointed special $\mathcal F$-spaces over $\underline\mathcal N$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:self-chain-ell}
\underline\mathcal B \ \stackrel{p}{\twoheadleftarrow} \quad \underline\mathcal B_1 \quad \stackrel{\phi}{\to} \quad \underline\mathcal B_2  
\quad \stackrel{j}{\hookleftarrow}  \ \quad \underline\mathcal B
\end{equation}
and a  map of $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibrations $p^*(\underline\mathcal E) \to j_!(\underline\mathcal E^\prime)$ covering $\phi: \underline\mathcal B_1 \to \underline\mathcal B_2$
(with sections)
$$p^*(\underline\mathcal E) \quad \to \quad \iota_!(\underline\mathcal E^\prime),$$
where $p$ is a cofibrant replacement of $\underline\mathcal B$, $j$ is a fibrant replacement of $\underline\mathcal B^\prime$,  
and the image of (\ref{eqn:self-chain-ell})
 in $Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(\underline\mathcal B,\underline\mathcal B)$ is the identity.

We denote by $X_\ell{\text -}fib(\underline\mathcal B)$ the set of equivalence classes of $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibrations over $\underline\mathcal B$.
<!-- end defn-13 -->
<!-- prop-12: body_md -->
\label{prop:functor-ell}
 Consider a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$ which is smash $\ell$-good.
 Sending a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$ to $X_\ell{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B)$
 and using bijections of $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete analogs of Lemmas \ref{lem:iota!}.and \ref{lem:p*}, 
 we obtain the functor
 $$X_\ell{\text-}fib(-): Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)^{op} \quad \to \quad (sets). $$
 
 Consequently, sending $\phi: \underline\mathcal B \dashrightarrow \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X)$ to $\phi^*(\pi_{X,\ell}) \in X{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B)$
 determines a well defined natural transformation of functors
 \begin{equation}
 \label{eqn:upper-ell-X}
 (-)^*(\pi_{X,\ell}): Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(-,\underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X)) \quad \to \quad X_\ell{\text-}fib(-).
 \end{equation}
<!-- end prop-12 -->
<!-- defn-14: body_md -->
\label{defn:principal-ell-simplex}
Assume that $X$ is a pointed, connected simplicial set which is smash $\ell$-good.
Let $f: \underline\mathcal E \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B$ be an $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration with $\underline\mathcal B$ a
pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space over $\underline\mathcal N$, 
and denote by $(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(f): (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(\underline\mathcal E) \to \underline\mathcal B$ the fiberwise 
$\mathbb Z/\ell$-completion of $f$.  Consider some $I = (i_,\ldots,i_n) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$.   
We say that a map $\phi: X^I \times \Delta[d]
 \to (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(\underline\mathcal E_I)$ is a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete principal $d$-simplex of $f$ provided that
 $\phi$ satisfies the following conditions.
 \begin{enumerate}
 \item
 The composition $f_I \circ \phi: X^I\times \Delta[d] \to (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(\underline\mathcal E_I) \to \underline\mathcal B_I$ factors though the
 projection, $X^I\times \Delta[d] \to \Delta[d] \to \underline\mathcal B_I$  and determines a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-equivalence \\
 $X^I \times \Delta[d] \ \to \ (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(\underline\mathcal E_I) \times_{\underline\mathcal B_I} \Delta[d]$.
 \item
  For every non-decreasing map $\alpha: {\bf n} \to {\bf r}$ in $\mathcal F$,  set 
  $J \equiv \alpha(I) = (j_1,\ldots,j_r) \in \underline\mathcal N(r), \ j_s = \sum_{\alpha(i) = s} i_s$.
 Then $\phi$ induces $\phi_\alpha: X^{\alpha(I)} \times \Delta[d] \to (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(\underline\mathcal E_{\alpha(I)})$ 
 fitting in the commutative square
 \begin{equation}
\xymatrix{
X^I \times \Delta[d] \ar[d]_{\alpha} \ar[r]^-\phi &  (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(\underline\mathcal E_I) \ar[d]^{\alpha} \\
X^J \times \Delta[d] \ar[r]^-{\phi^\alpha} & (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(\underline\mathcal E_J)
}
\end{equation}
such that $\phi^\alpha$ induces a mod-$\ell$ equivalence 
$$X^J \times \Delta[d] \ \to \ (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(\underline\mathcal E_J)  
\times_{\underline\mathcal B_J} \Delta[d].$$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end defn-14 -->
<!-- construct-2: body_md -->
\label{construct:principal-ell-spaces}
Assume that $X$ is a pointed, connected simplicial set which is smash $\ell$-good and consider 
some $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration $f: \underline\mathcal E \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B$ with $\underline\mathcal B$ a 
pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space over $\underline\mathcal N$.
There is a commutative diagram of simplicial sets
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:2-sided-ell}
\xymatrix{
 (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(\underline\mathcal E_I) \ar[d]_{f_I}  & \ar[l]_-{\tilde p_{f,I}}  B(P_\ell(f_I),G_\ell(X^I),X_\ell^I )
