 \ar[d]^{\pi_{P_\ell,X,\ell,I}} \ar[r]^-{\tilde q_{f,I}}  &   
 B(G_\ell(X_\ell^I),X^I ) \ar[d]^{\pi_{X,\ell,I}} &  \\
\underline\mathcal B_I   & \ar[l]^-{p_{f,I}}  B(P_\ell(f_I),G_\ell(X^I)) \ar[r]_-{q_{f,I}} &  BG_\ell(X^I)
}
\end{equation}
for each $I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$, where the right horizontal maps are projections, the lower left map
is given by projection to $P_\ell(f_I)$ followed by the structure map
$P_\ell(f_I) \to \underline\mathcal B_I$.  The upper left map $\tilde p_{f,I}$ utilizes the right action of $G_\ell(X^I)$ 
on $P_\ell(f_I)$ and the left action of $G_\ell(X^I)$ on $(X^I)_\ell$.  The formulation of $\tilde p_{f,I}$
is exactly parallel to the formulation the upper left arrow of (\ref{eqn:2-sided}) given in the proof
of Proposition \ref{prop:2-sided}.

The vertical maps of (\ref{eqn:2-sided-ell}) are fibrations whose homotopy fibers are homotopy equivalent to $(X^I)_\ell$, the
squares of (\ref{eqn:2-sided-ell}) are homotopy cartesian, 
and the left square is a homotopy
equivalence of fibrations (see the proof of Proposition \ref{prop:2-sided}).
<!-- end construct-2 -->
<!-- prop-13: body_md -->
\label{prop:func-P(f)-ell}
Consider a pointed, connected simplicial set $X$ which is smash $\ell$-good.
Let  $f: \underline\mathcal E \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B$ be a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration with $\underline\mathcal B$ a
pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space over $\underline\mathcal N$.
The diagrams of (\ref{eqn:2-sided-ell}) for all $I \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n}), n > 0$ determine
a commutative diagram of $\mathcal F$-spaces
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:2-sided-func-ell}
\xymatrix{
 \underline\mathcal E  \ar[r]^-\epsilon \ar[d]_f & (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(\underline\mathcal E)  \ar[d]^{(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(f)} &
 \ar[l]_-{\tilde p_{f,\ell}} \underline\mathcal B (P_\ell(f),G_\ell(X),X_\ell) \ar[d]_{\pi_{P_\ell(f),X_\ell}} \ar[r]^-{\tilde q_{f,\ell}} &   
 \underline\mathcal B (G_\ell(X),X_\ell) \ar[d]_{\pi_{X,\ell}}   \\
\underline\mathcal B \ar[r]^= & \underline\mathcal B   & \ar[l]^-{p_{f,\ell}} \underline\mathcal B (P_\ell(f),G_\ell(X)) \ar[r]_-{q_{f,\ell}} &  \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X)
}
\end{equation}
whose squares are maps of $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibrations and whose left and middle horizontal maps are weak equivalences.

In other words, (\ref{eqn:2-sided-func-ell}) determines a homotopy class of maps of 
$\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibrations  $f \to \pi_{X_\ell}$.
<!-- end prop-13 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
The map $\epsilon: \underline\mathcal E   \to (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(\underline\mathcal E)$
appearing in the upper left corner of (\ref{eqn:2-sided-func-ell}) is induced by the natural 
transformation $id(-) \to  (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(-)$ determining a fiber homotopy equivalence 
$f \to (\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty^\bullet(f)$ by Proposition \ref{prop:X-ell-cases}(2). 

The middle horizontal 
maps determine weak homotopy equivalences because each $(P_\ell(f))_I \to \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X)_I$
is a ``principal $G_\ell(X^I)$"-fibration.  By inspection, we see that the right horizontal arrows
determine a map of $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completed $X$-fibrations.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- thm-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:X-ell-universal}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set which is smash $\ell$-good.
The natural transformation of functors from 
 $Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)^{op}$  to the category  of sets
 \begin{equation}
(-)^*(\pi_{X,\ell}):  Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(-, \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X)) \quad \to \quad X_\ell{\text-}fib(-) 
\end{equation}
sending $\phi: \underline\mathcal B \dashrightarrow \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X)$ to  $\phi^*(\pi_{X,\ell})$
in $X_\ell{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B)$ is an isomorphism of functors with inverse the natural transformation
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:rho-ell-map}
\rho_{X,\ell}: X_\ell{\text-}fib(-) \quad \to \quad Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*})(-, \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X))
\end{equation}
sending the equivalence class in $X_\ell{\text-}fib(\underline\mathcal B)$ of a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$  
 to the homotopy class of the composition 
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:rho-ell}
\rho_{X,\ell}(f) \quad \equiv \quad q_{f ,\ell}\circ p_{f,\ell}^{-1}: \underline\mathcal B \dashrightarrow \underline\mathcal B G(X)
\end{equation}
 as in (\ref{eqn:2-sided-func-ell}).

If $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ is a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration with $\underline\mathcal B$ a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space over $\underline\mathcal N$, 
then a representative of $\rho_{X,\ell}(f)$ is called a classifying map for $f$.
<!-- end thm-6 -->
<!-- prop-14: body_md -->
\label{prop:relate}
Consider be a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space   $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$,
let $f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B$ be an $X$-fibration over $\underline\mathcal B$, and consider the fiber-wise $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completion,
 $\tilde f \ \equiv \ (\mathbb Z/\ell)^\bullet_\infty(f): \widetilde{\underline\mathcal E} \to \underline\mathcal B$.  Then the (homotopy type of the)
 classifying map 
$\rho_{X,\ell}(\tilde f):  \underline\mathcal B \ \dashrightarrow \ \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X)$ for $(\mathbb Z/\ell)^\bullet_\infty(f)$ 
equals $\iota_\ell \circ \rho_X(f): \underline\mathcal B \ \dashrightarrow \ \underline\mathcal B G(X) \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X).$ 

Consequently, the following square of functors 
 \begin{equation}
\xymatrix{
Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(-,\underline\mathcal B G(X)) \ar[d]_-{\iota_\ell \circ(-)} \ar[rr]^-{(-)^*(\pi_X))}
& & X{\text -}fib(-) \ar[d]^{(\mathbb Z/\ell)^\bullet_\infty(-)} \\
Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(-,\underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X))  \ar[rr]^-{(-)^*(\pi_{X,\ell)}}
& & X_\ell{\text -}fib(-) 
}
\end{equation}
is commutative.
<!-- end prop-14 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
\label{rem:ell-local}
The discussion of this section culminating in Theorem \ref{thm:X-ell-universal} applies
essentially verbatim to the ``Bousfield-Kan localization at $\ell$".   Namely, we simply 
replace $(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty(-)$ by $(\mathbb Z_{(\ell)}) _\infty(-)$ and $(-)_\ell$ by $(-)_{(\ell)}$.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- defn-15: body_md -->
\label{defn:orient}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set satisfying the condition that $\Sigma_n \subset G^o(X^n)$
(i.e., that permuting factors
of the smash product is weakly homotopy equivalent to the identity) for each $n > 0$.  Consider a
pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$.
An  $X$-fibration of $\mathcal F$-spaces, $(f:\underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B, \ s: \underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal E)$ is said
to be oriented if its classifying map $\rho_X(f): \underline\mathcal B \ \dashrightarrow \ {\underline\mathcal B}G(X)$
is equipped with a factorization of the form $\iota \circ \rho_X^o(f): \underline\mathcal B \ \dashrightarrow \ {\underline\mathcal B}G^o(X) \to \ {\underline\mathcal B}G(X).$

We define $X{\text -}fib^o(\underline\mathcal B)$ to be equivalence class of oriented $X$-fibrations, where we set the 
oriented $X$-fibrations $f, \ f^\prime$  equivalent if their factorizations $ \rho_X^o(f), \ 
 \rho_X^o(f^\prime)$ are equal in $Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(-, \underline\mathcal B G(X))$.
<!-- end defn-15 -->
<!-- cor-4: body_md -->
\label{cor:X-fib-o}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set satisfying the condition that $\Sigma_n \subset G^o(X^n)$.
Then the following natural transformations are mutually inverse:
\begin{equation}
(-)^*(\pi^o_X):  Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(-, \underline\mathcal B G^o(X)) \quad \to \quad X{\text-}fib^o(-),
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:nat-trans-o}
\rho^o_X:  X{\text-}fib^o(-) \quad \to \quad Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*})(-, \underline\mathcal B G^o(X)).
\end{equation}
<!-- end cor-4 -->
<!-- defn-16: body_md -->
\label{defn:orient-ell}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set satisfying the condition that $\Sigma_n \subset G^o(X^n)$
and assume further that $X$ is smash $\ell$-good.  Consider a
pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space $\underline\mathcal B$ over $\underline\mathcal N$.
Then a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration $(f: \underline\mathcal E \to \underline\mathcal B, \ s: \underline\mathcal B \to \underline\mathcal E)$  
 is said to be oriented if its classifying map $\rho_{X,\ell}(f): \underline\mathcal B \ \dashrightarrow \ {\underline\mathcal B}G_\ell(X)$
is equipped with a factorization of the form 
$\iota \circ \rho^o_{X,\ell}(f): \underline\mathcal B \ \dashrightarrow \ {\underline\mathcal B}G_\ell^o(X) \to \ {\underline\mathcal B}G_\ell(X).$

We define $X_\ell{\text -}fib^o(\underline\mathcal B)$ to be equivalence class of oriented $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibrations, where we set the 
oriented $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibrations $f, \ f^\prime$  equivalent if their factorizations $ \rho^o_{X,\ell}(f), \ 
 \rho^o_{X,\ell}(f^\prime)$ are equal in $Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(-, \underline\mathcal B G_\ell^o(X))$.
<!-- end defn-16 -->
<!-- cor-5: body_md -->
\label{cor:X-fib-o-ell}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set satisfying the condition that $\Sigma_n \subset G^o(X^n)$
and assume further that $X$ is smash $\ell$-good. 
Then the following natural transformations are mutually inverse:
\begin{equation}
(-)^*(\pi^o_{X,\ell}):  Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(-, \underline\mathcal B G_\ell^o(X)) \quad \to \quad X_\ell{\text-}fib^o(-),
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:nat-trans-ell}
\rho^o_{X,\ell}:  X_\ell{\text-}fib^o(-) \quad \to \quad Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*})(-, \underline\mathcal B G_\ell^o(X)).
\end{equation}
<!-- end cor-5 -->
<!-- cor-6: body_md -->
\label{cor:relate-o}
Let $X$ be a pointed, connected simplicial set which is smash $\ell$-good
and consider a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space
$\underline\mathcal B$ which satisfies the condition that $\pi_1(\underline\mathcal B_I) = 0$ 
for all $I \in \underline\mathcal N(\bf n)$, all $n > 0$. 
Let $f: \underline\mathcal E \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B$ be an oriented $X$-fibration with orientation
$\rho^o_X(f):  \underline\mathcal B  \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B G^o(X)$.
Then $(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty(f): \underline\mathcal E_\ell \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B_\ell$
is a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration with orientation
$$\lambda \circ (\rho^o_X(f))_\ell:  \underline\mathcal B_\ell \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B G^o(X)_\ell \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B G^o_\ell(X).$$

Let $(\mathcal F{\text -}\widetilde{spec}/\underline\mathcal N)_*$ denote the full subcategory of pointed-connected 
special $\mathcal F$-spaces $\underline\mathcal B$ with the property that $\pi_1(\underline\mathcal B_I) = 0$ 
for all $I \in \underline\mathcal N(\bf n)$, all $n > 0$.  Then the following square of contravariant functors
from $Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}\widetilde{spec}/\underline\mathcal N)_*)$ to (sets)
 \begin{equation}
\xymatrix{
Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}\widetilde{spec}/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}(-,\underline\mathcal B G^o(X)) \ar[d]_-{\lambda \circ(-)_\ell} \ar[rr]^-{(-)^*(\pi^o_X))}
& & X{\text -}fib^o(-) \ar[d]^{(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty(-)} \\
Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}\widetilde{spec}/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}((-)_\ell,\underline\mathcal B G^o_\ell(X))  \ar[rr]^-{(-)^*(\pi^o_{X,\ell}))}
& & X_\ell{\text -}fib^o(-) 
}
\end{equation}
commutes.
<!-- end cor-6 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
By Proposition \ref{prop:X-ell-cases}(3), $(\mathbb Z/\ell)_\infty(f)$ is a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration.
The fact that the classifying map for this  $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibration is oriented by
$\lambda \circ (\rho^o_X(f))_\ell$ follows from the commutativity of the following diagram
of $\mathcal F$-spaces
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:comm-cF-2}
\xymatrix{
 (\underline\mathcal E)_\ell \ar[d]_{f_\ell} \ar[r] & \mathcal B (G^o(X),X)_\ell \ar[r]  \ar[d]^{(\pi_{X,o})_\ell} &  \underline \mathcal B (G^o_\ell(X),X_\ell) 
\ar[d]^{\pi_{X,\ell,o}} \ar[r] & \mathcal B (G_\ell(X),X_\ell) \ar[d]^{\pi_{X,\ell}}\\
\underline\mathcal B_\ell \ar[r]_-{(\phi_f)_\ell} & \underline \mathcal B G^o(X)_\ell \ar[r]_{{\lambda}} & \underline \mathcal B G^o_\ell(X) \ar[r] & \underline \mathcal B G_\ell(X) .
}
\end{equation}
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- prop-15: body_md -->
\label{prop:fingen}  \cite[Prop 7.1]{Bo-Kan}
Let $X$ be a finite, nilpotent simplicial set.
\begin{enumerate}
\item
The homotopy groups of $G^o(X)$ are finitely generated abelian groups.
\item
The map $\pi_*(G^o(X)) \ \to \ \pi_*((G^o(X)_\ell)$ is given by tensoring with $\mathbb Z_\ell$.
\item
The map $\pi_*(G^o(X)) \ \to \ \pi_*(G_\ell^o(X))$ is given by tensoring with $\mathbb Z_\ell$.
\item
The natural map $(G^o(X))_\ell \ \to \ G^o_\ell(X)$ is a homotopy equivalence.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end prop-15 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Assertion (1) is  a well known consequence of the fundamental theorem of J.-P. Serre \cite{Serre}
concerning the homotopy groups of spheres.  (See \cite[Prop 7.1(i]{Bo-Kan}.)
Assertion (2) is given by \cite[Ex VI.5.2]{Bo-Kan}.

Assertion (3) is given by \cite[Prop VI.7.1.(iii)]{Bo-Kan}
Together, assertions (2) and (3) imply that the natural map $(G^o(X))_\ell \ \to \ G^o_\ell(X)$ is a
weak equivalence.  Since this is a map of Kan complexes, it is a homotopy equivalence.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- cor-7: body_md -->
\label{cor:cover}
Let $X$ be a finite nilpotent simplicial set.
If $\Sigma_n \subset G^o(X^n)$ for all $n>0$,
then the natural map \ $\mathcal B G^o(X)  \to \mathcal B G^o_\ell(X)$ \ induces a homotopy equivalence
of $\mathcal F$-spaces over $\underline \mathcal N$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:S2-hom-equiv}
\lambda: \underline \mathcal B G^o(X)_\ell \quad \to \quad  \underline \mathcal B G^o_\ell(X).
\end{equation}

Moreover, the natural map \ $\underline\mathcal B G^o(X),X)_\ell \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B(G_\ell^o(X),X_\ell)$ \ covers $\lambda$,
determining a map of oriented $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $\mathcal F$-spaces 
$$(\pi_X^o)_\ell: \underline\mathcal B G^o(X),X)_\ell \to \mathcal B G^o(X)  \quad \to \quad \pi_{X,\ell}^o: \underline\mathcal B(G_\ell^o(X),X_\ell) \to \underline \mathcal B G^o_\ell(X).$$
<!-- end cor-7 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
The first assertion follows from Proposition \ref{prop:fingen}(4).  The second assertion
follows from Proposition \ref{prop:X-ell-cases}(3) and the fact that each $BG^o(X^I)$
is simply connected.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- prop-16: body_md -->
\label{prop:S2-spec}
The homotopy type of the map $||\underline\mathcal B G^o(S^m)_\ell||^+ \  \to \ ||\underline\mathcal B G_\ell(S^m)||^+$
of $\Omega$-spectra is independent of $m>0$, and we denote this by 
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:GS}
(\bf{BG^o(S)})_\ell \quad \to \quad   \bf{BG_\ell(S)}.
 \end{equation}
 The  sequence of $0$-connected $\Omega$-spectra
 \begin{equation}
\label{eqn:fib-seq}
(\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_\ell \quad \to \quad   \bf{BG_\ell(S)}_{[0]} \quad \to \quad {\bf K}(\mathbb Z_\ell^*,1)
 \end{equation}
 is a fiber sequence of $\Omega$-spectra.
<!-- end prop-16 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
As shown in \cite[\S 4]{Segal}, the natural maps
$$\underline\mathcal B G^o_\ell(S^m)({\bf 1}) \ = \ (\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0} B G^o_\ell(S^{mn}) \ \to \ (\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0} B G^o_\ell(S^{mn}))^+,$$
$$\underline\mathcal B G_\ell(S^m)({\bf 1}) \ = \ (\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0} B G_\ell(S^{mn}) \ \to \ (\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0} B G_\ell(S^{mn}))^+$$
induce the maps $(-)\otimes_{\mathbb Z/\ell[\mathbb N]} \mathbb Z/\ell[\mathbb Z]$ in mod-$\ell$ homology and thus induce 
homotopy equivalences of $\ell$-complete spaces
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:ell-compl}
\varinjlim_n B G^o_\ell(S^{n\cdot m}) \ \stackrel{\approx}{\to} \ (\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0} BG^o_\ell(S^{mn}))^+_{[0]}, \quad
\varinjlim_n B G_\ell(S^{n\cdot m}) \ \stackrel{\approx}{\to}  \ (\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0} B G_\ell(S^{mn}))^+_{[0]}.
\end{equation}
Observe that the natural maps (given by suspensions) 
$\varinjlim_n B G^o_\ell(S^n) \to \varinjlim_n B G^o_\ell(S^{n\cdot m})$ and 
$\varinjlim_n B G_\ell(S^n) \to \varinjlim_n B G_\ell(S^{n\cdot m})$ are isomorphisms, so that  
the homotopy type of the map of 0-connected spectra
$||\underline\mathcal B G^o(S^m)_\ell||^+_{[0]} \  \to \ ||\underline\mathcal B G_\ell(S^m)||_{[0]}^+$ is independent of $m$ for $m > 0$.

Since the map $G_\ell(S^n) \to G_\ell(S^{n+1})$ induced by suspension induces the
identity map $\mathbb Z_\ell^* = \pi_0(G_\ell(S^n)) \ \to \  \pi_0(G_\ell(S^{n+1})) = \mathbb Z_\ell^*$ for $n > 0$,
we conclude that 
$$ \varinjlim_n (B G^o(S^{n\cdot m}))_\ell \ \stackrel{\approx}{\to} \ 
\varinjlim_n B G^o_\ell(S^{n\cdot m}) \ \to \ \varinjlim_n B G_\ell(S^{n\cdot m})  \ \to \ K(\mathbb Z_\ell^*,1)$$
is a fiber sequence.  This implies that 
$$||\underline\mathcal B G^o(S^m)_\ell||^+_{[0]} \ \stackrel{\approx}{\to} \ 
||\underline\mathcal B G_\ell^o(S^m_\ell)||^+_{[0]} \quad \to \quad ||\underline\mathcal B G_\ell(S^m)||^+_{[0]} \quad \to  {\bf K}(\mathbb Z_\ell^*,1)$$
is a fiber sequence of 0-connected $\Omega$-spectra as asserted.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- cor-8: body_md -->
\label{cor:S-hom}
The homotopy groups \ $\pi_{i+1}((\bf{BG^o(S)})_\ell)  \ = \ \pi_{i+1}(\bf{BG_\ell(S)})$ \
can be naturally identified with the $\ell$-adic completion
of the $i$-th stable homotopy groups of the spheres for $i > 0$, whereas $\pi_1((\bf{BG^o(S)})_\ell)) = 0$
and $\pi_1(\bf{BG_\ell(S)}) = \mathbb Z_\ell^*$.
<!-- end cor-8 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:cJ}
For each $n > 0$, the topological action of $GL_n(\mathbb C)^{top}$ on $\mathbb C^+$ determines an embedding
$GL_n(\mathbb C)^{top} \hookrightarrow G^{top,o}(\mathbb C^{n+})$, where $G^{top,o}(\mathbb C^{n+})$
is the topological group of oriented self homotopy equivalences of $\mathbb C^{n+}$.   For each $n > 0$ and 
each $I= (i_1,\ldots i_n) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$, these maps induce maps of fibrations
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:fiber-XI}
\xymatrix{
Sin({\underline \mathcal B}(GL(\mathbb C),\mathbb C^+)^{top})_I \ar[r] \ar[d]_{\tau_{\mathbb C^+}} 
\ar[r] \ar@/^2pc/[rr]
& \underline Sin(\mathcal B (G^{top,o}(\mathbb C^+),\mathbb C^+))_I \ar[d]^{\pi_{\mathbb C^+}^o} \ar[r] &
 \underline Sin(\mathcal B (G^{top}(\mathbb C^+),\mathbb C^+))_I \ar[d]^{\pi_{\mathbb C^+}}\\
Sin({\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_I \ar[r]^{\mathcal J^{o}} \ar@/_2pc/[rr]_{\mathcal J} & Sin(\underline\mathcal B G^{top,o}(\mathbb C^+))_I \ar[r]^{\iota} 
& Sin(\underline\mathcal B G^{top}(\mathbb C^+))_I.
}
\end{equation}
whose induced maps on fibers are homotopic to the identity map of $|X^I |$.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- prop-17: body_md -->
Let $X$ be a finite, pointed simplicial set with $|X| \simeq S^2$.  Then $\tau_{\mathbb C^+}$ of (\ref{eqn:tau-top-spec})
fits in a commutative diagram of $\mathcal F$-spaces
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:cJ}
\xymatrix{
Sin({\underline \mathcal B}(GL(\mathbb C),\mathbb C^+)^{top}) \ar[r] \ar[d]_{\tau_{\mathbb C^+}} 
\ar[r] \ar@/^2pc/[rr]
& \underline Sin(\mathcal B (G^{top,o}(\mathbb C^+),\mathbb C^+)) \ar[d]^{\pi_{\mathbb C^+}^o} \ar[r] &
 \underline Sin(\mathcal B (G^{top}(\mathbb C^+),\mathbb C^+)) \ar[d]^{\pi_{\mathbb C^+}}\\
Sin({\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top}) \ar[r]^{\mathcal J^{o}} \ar@/_2pc/[rr]_{\mathcal J} & Sin(\underline\mathcal B G^{top,o}(\mathbb C^+)) \ar[r]^{\iota} & Sin(\underline\mathcal B G^{top}(\mathbb C^+))
}
\end{equation}
whose vertical maps are $X$-fibrations and whose squares are Cartesian.

Consequently, $\tau_{\mathbb C^+}$ is an oriented $X$ fibration oriented by
$$adj^{-1}\circ \mathcal J^o: Sin({\underline \mathcal B}(GL(\mathbb C))^{top}) \ \dashrightarrow \  \underline\mathcal B G^o(X),$$
where $adj: \underline\mathcal B G(X) \to Sin(\underline\mathcal B G(|X|)^{top})$ is the weak homotopy equivalence appearing in (\ref{eqn:compat-I}).
<!-- end prop-17 -->
<!-- notation-2: body_md -->
\label{note:J}
Let $X$ be a finite, pointed simplicial set with $|X| \simeq S^2$.  We denote by \quad \quad 
$ {\bf J^o}: {\bf B GL(C)} \quad \to \quad {\bf BG^o(S)}$ \quad \quad
the homotopy class of maps of connective $\Omega$-spectra 
determined by the map 
$$(adj^o)^{-1}\circ \mathcal J^o: Sin({\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top})  \dashrightarrow \underline\mathcal B G^o(X) \ {\text in} \ Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)$$
in (\ref{eqn:cJ}).

We set \quad \quad 
${\bf J} \ \equiv \ \iota \circ  {\bf J^o}: \ {\bf B GL(C)} \quad \to \quad {\bf BG(S)}.$
<!-- end notation-2 -->
<!-- construct-3: body_md -->
\label{construct:base-change}
Let $R$ be either $\mathbb C$ or $W(k)$ or $k$.
There is a natural  map of $\mathcal F$-objects of pointed simplicial $R$-schemes
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:tau-alg}
\tau_R: {\underline \mathcal B}(GL_R,S_R^{2,alg}) \quad \to \quad {\underline \mathcal B}GL_R \ .
\end{equation}
The evaluation at ${\bf 1} \in \mathcal F$ of $\tau_R$ determines the natural projection 
$$ \coprod_{n\geq 0} B(GL_{R,n},S_R^{2n,alg}) \quad \to \quad \coprod_{n\geq 0} BGL_{R,n} \ .$$

Base changes along $\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits \mathbb C \to \operatorname{Spec}\nolimits W(k)$ and along $\operatorname{Spec}\nolimits k \to \operatorname{Spec}\nolimits W$ determine a commutative
diagram of simplicial schemes
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:base-change-s}
\xymatrix{
{\underline\mathcal B}(GL_\mathbb C,S^{2,alg}_\mathbb C) \ar[d]_{\tau_{\mathbb C}} \ar[r] & {\underline\mathcal B}(GL_{W(k)},S^{2,alg}_{W(k)})  
\ar[d]^{\tau_{W(k)}}  & \ar[l] {\underline\mathcal B}(GL_k,S^{2,alg}_k) \ar[d]^{\tau_k} \\
\underline\mathcal B GL_\mathbb C \ar[r] & \underline\mathcal B GL_{W(k)} &  \ar[l] \underline\mathcal B GL_k \ .
}
\end{equation}
<!-- end construct-3 -->
<!-- prop-18: body_md -->
\label{prop:class-compare}
The following commutative diagram of $\mathcal F$-spaces 
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:C-to-Cwedge}
\xymatrix{
Sin( {\underline \mathcal B} (GL(\mathbb C),\mathbb C^+)^{top})_\ell \ar[d]_{(\tau_{\mathbb C^+})_\ell}  \ar[r]^{\tilde \gamma} &
Sin({\underline \mathcal B} (GL(\mathbb C),|S^{2,alg}_\mathbb C|)^{top})_\ell \ar[r] \ar[d]^{(\tau_{|S^{2,alg}_\mathbb C|})_\ell} &
({\underline\mathcal B} (GL_{\mathbb C},S_\mathbb C^{2,alg}))^\wedge \ar[d]_{(\tau_\mathbb C)^\wedge}    
 \\
 Sin({\underline \mathcal B} GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell  \ar[r]^= & Sin(\underline\mathcal B GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell \ar[r]_{f_{top\mapsto \mathbb C}} &
({\underline\mathcal B} GL_{\mathbb C})^\wedge.
}
\end{equation}
consists of squares which are maps of oriented $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $S^2$-fibrations with horizontal maps
which are weak equivalences.
Here, the lower horizontal arrow  $f_{top \mapsto \mathbb C}$ is the homotopy class of $f_{top \mapsto \mathbb C}:  
Sin({\underline \mathcal B} GL(\mathbb C))^{top})_\ell \ \dashrightarrow \ ({\underline\mathcal B} GL_{\mathbb C})^\wedge$ in $Ho(\mathcal F[s.sets_*])$
specified by the functorial
``short chain"  of homotopy equivalences $\rho_{U\mapsto X}, \ \rho_{top \mapsto \pi_0}$ of Theorem \ref{thm:holim}.
<!-- end prop-18 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
The compatibility of $\gamma_n\times \gamma_n$
with smash products (see (\ref{eqn:equiv})) together with the explicit descriptions of the $\mathcal F$-spaces
(see Example \ref{ex:BGL}) provides the necessary functoriality with respect to 
maps in $\mathcal F$  to yield the commutativity of the left square of (\ref{eqn:C-to-Cwedge}).
The compatibility established in Proposition \ref{prop:map-cone} of the actions of $GL_n(C)^{top}$ 
on $|S^{2n,alg}|$ and on $(\mathbb C^n)^+$ utilizing the maps 
$ \gamma_n: |S^{2n,alg}_\mathbb C| \to (\mathbb C^n)^+$ of (\ref{eqn:tau-n}) imply that the left square of (\ref{eqn:C-to-Cwedge})
is a fiber homotopy equivalences.

The commutativity of the right square of (\ref{eqn:C-to-Cwedge}) follows from the naturality 
of $\rho_{U\mapsto X}, \ \rho_{top \mapsto \pi_0}$.
 This square is a homotopy equivalence of $S^2$-spaces by  
 the ``classical comparison theorem" in \'etale homotopy as in (\ref{eqn:Phi_X}). 
 
 By Corollary \ref{cor:relate-o}, $(\tau_{\mathbb C^+})_\ell$ is oriented by $\lambda \circ ((adj^o)^{-1}\circ \mathcal J^o)_\ell$.
 We define the orientations of $(\tau_{|S^{2,alg}_\mathbb C|})_\ell$ and $(\tau_\mathbb C)^\wedge$ as those determined
 by $(\tau_{\mathbb C^+})_\ell$: namely, \\
 $\lambda \circ ((adj^o)^{-1}\circ \mathcal J^o)_\ell$ and 
$\lambda \circ ((adj^o)^{-1}\circ \mathcal J^o)_\ell \circ (f_{top\mapsto \mathbb C}^{-1}$).
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- prop-19: body_md -->
\label{prop:tau-hat}
By applying $(-)^\wedge$ to (\ref{eqn:base-change-s}) in Construction \ref{construct:base-change} we 
obtain the following commutative diagram of $\mathcal F$-spaces
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:base-change-ss}
\xymatrix{
(\underline\mathcal B (GL_\mathbb C,S^{2,alg}_\mathbb C))^\wedge \ar[d]_{(\tau_{\mathbb C})^\wedge} \ar[r] & (\underline\mathcal B (GL_{W(k)},S^{2,alg}_{W(k)}))^\wedge  
\ar[d]^{(\tau_{W(k)})^\wedge}  & \ar[l] (\underline\mathcal B (GL_k,S^{2,alg}_k))^\wedge \ar[d]^{(\tau_k)^\wedge} \\
(\underline\mathcal B GL_\mathbb C)^\wedge \ar[r] & (\underline\mathcal B GL_{W(k)})^\wedge &  \ar[l] (\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge \ .
}
\end{equation}
whose squares constitute maps of oriented $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibrations with $|X| = S^2$ 
and whose horizontal maps are weak equivalences.

We denote by $f_{\mathbb C\mapsto k}: (\underline\mathcal B GL_\mathbb C)^\wedge  \ \dashrightarrow \ (\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge$  the homotopy class
of maps of $\mathcal F$-spaces defined by the bottom row of (\ref{eqn:base-change-ss}).
<!-- end prop-19 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
The fact that the $\mathcal F$-spaces $({\underline \mathcal B}GL_R)^\wedge$ and $({\underline \mathcal B}(GL_R,S_R^{alg}))^\wedge$ 
are special $\mathcal F$-spaces follows from Corollary \ref{cor:product}.  The fact that the lower horizontal maps of
(\ref{eqn:base-change-ss}) are homotopy equivalences follows from Proposition \ref{prop:comparison}.

A functorial isomorphism is given in \cite[Thm 10.7]{F82} relating the mod-$\ell$ \'etale cohomology 
of the geometric fiber of  a map $X \to Y$ of simplicial $R$-schemes such as 
those occurring in Corollary \ref{cor:product} and the mod-$\ell$ cohomology 
of the homotopy-theoretic fiber of $X^\wedge \to Y^\wedge$.
This, together with the fact that each $(BGL_{n,R})^\wedge$ is simply connected, provides the 
functorial homotopy comparisons of the fibers of $(\underline\mathcal B(GL_R,S_R^{2,alg}))^\wedge_I \to 
(\underline\mathcal B GL_R)^\wedge_I$ with $(\prod_{j= 1}^n S^{2i_j})_\ell$ for any $I = (i_1,\ldots,i_n) \in  \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$
required for $(\tau_R)^\wedge$ to be a $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completed $S^2$-fibration.  
Consequently, the squares of (\ref{eqn:base-change-ss}) determine maps of $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $X$-fibrations.


By Proposition \ref{prop:comparison}, the horizontal maps of (\ref{eqn:base-change-ss}) are weak equivalences.

We give $(\tau_{W(k)})^\wedge$ and $(\tau_k)^\wedge$ the orientations determined by the orientation
of $(\tau_{\mathbb C})^\wedge$.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- defn-17: body_md -->
\label{defn:cJ-k}
Let $X$ be a finite, pointed simplicial set with $|X| \simeq S^2$.
 We define 
 $$(\mathcal J^o_k)^\wedge: (\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge \quad \dashrightarrow \quad \underline\mathcal B G^o_\ell(X)$$
 to be the 
homotopy class of the classifying map for the oriented $Z/\ell$-completed $S^2$-fibration $(\tau_k)^\wedge$
occurring in (\ref{eqn:base-change-ss}).
<!-- end defn-17 -->
<!-- prop-20: body_md -->
\label{prop:relate-Js}
Set \ $f_{top \mapsto k} \ \equiv \ f_{top \mapsto \mathbb C} \circ f_{\mathbb C \mapsto k}$ in $Ho(\mathcal F[s.sets_*]/\underline\mathcal N)$,
and set 
$$\mathcal J^o_\ell \ \equiv \ (-)_\ell \circ (adj)^{-1}\circ \mathcal J: Sin({\underline \mathcal B}GL(\mathbb C)^{top})  \dashrightarrow \underline\mathcal B G^o(X)  \ \to \ \underline\mathcal B G^o_\ell(X).$$
Then the maps  
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:equal-class}
\mathcal J_\ell^o, \ \  (\mathcal J_k)^\wedge \circ f_{top \mapsto k}:  Sin({\underline \mathcal B} GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell \ \dashrightarrow \  \mathcal B G_\ell(X)
\end{equation}
are equal in $Ho(\mathcal F[s.sets_*]/\underline\mathcal N)$.
<!-- end prop-20 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
The asserted equality follows immediately from the observation that 
$(\mathcal J_k)^\wedge$ can be represented using Propositions \ref{prop:class-compare} and \ref{prop:tau-hat}
as the composition of maps in $Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:string}
(\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge \ \stackrel{f_{\mathbb C\mapsto k}}{\dashleftarrow} \   (\underline\mathcal B GL_\mathbb C)^\wedge  \
 \stackrel{f_{top\mapsto \mathbb C}}{\dashleftarrow} Sin({\underline \mathcal B} GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell \
\stackrel{ \mathcal J^o_\ell}{\dashrightarrow} \  \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X).
\end{equation}
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- thm-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:Sul}
Let $\sigma_{\mathbb C} \in Gal(\mathbb C/\mathbb Q)$ be an extension to a discontinuous automorphism of $\mathbb C$ of the Teichm\"uller lifting
$\sigma_{W(k)}: W(k) \to W(k)$ of the arithmetic Frobenius $\sigma \in Gal(k/\mathbb F_p)$.  
 The following commutative diagram in $\mathcal F[s.sets_*]$ (with horizontal maps which are
homotopy equivalences)
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:sigma} 
\xymatrix{
Sin(\underline \mathcal B GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell  \ar[rr]^-{f_{top\mapsto \mathbb C}}  & & (\underline\mathcal B GL_\mathbb C)^\wedge \ar[d]^{(\sigma_\mathbb C^{-1})^\wedge} 
\ar[rr]^-{f_{\mathbb C \mapsto k}} & & ({\underline\mathcal B}GL_k)^\wedge \ar[d]^{F^\wedge} \\
Sin(\underline \mathcal B GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell  \ar[rr]_-{f_{top\mapsto \mathbb C}} & & (\underline\mathcal B GL_{\mathbb C})^\wedge  \ar[rr]^-{f_{\mathbb C \mapsto k}} & & ({\underline\mathcal B}GL_k)^\wedge 
}
\end{equation}
determines a homotopy commutative diagram of associated connective spectra upon applying the functor $(||-||)$
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:sigma-||} 
\xymatrix{
||Sin(\underline \mathcal B GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell || \ar[d]_{(\psi^p)_\ell} \ar[rr]^-{f_{top\mapsto \mathbb C}}  & & ||(\underline\mathcal B GL_\mathbb C)^\wedge|| 
\ar[d]^{(\sigma_\mathbb C^{-1})^\wedge} 
\ar[rr]^-{f_{\mathbb C \mapsto k}} & & ||({\underline\mathcal B}GL_k)^\wedge||^{+} \ar[d]^{F^\wedge} \\
||(Sin(\underline \mathcal B GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell ||  \ar[rr]_-{f_{top\mapsto \mathbb C}} & & ||(\underline\mathcal B GL_{\mathbb C})^\wedge||^{+}  \ar[rr]^-{f_{\mathbb C \mapsto k}} 
& & ||({\underline\mathcal B}GL_k)^\wedge||
}
\end{equation}
<!-- end thm-7 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
We consider the commutative diagram of $\mathcal F$-spaces over $\underline \mathcal N$:
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:T-GL}
\xymatrix@C6pc{
Sin(\underline\mathcal B T(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell   \ar@/_4pc/[ddd]_{(-)^p} \ar[d] \ar[r]^{f_{top\mapsto \mathbb C}} & (\underline\mathcal B T_{\mathbb C})^\wedge \ar[d] \ar@/^4pc/[ddd]^{(-)^p} \\
Sin(\underline \mathcal B GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell  \ar[d]^{(\psi^p)_\ell} \ar[r]^{f_{top\mapsto \mathbb C}} & (\underline\mathcal B GL_{\mathbb C}))^\wedge \ar[d]_{(\sigma_\mathbb C^{-1})^\wedge}\\
Sin(\underline \mathcal B GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell   \ar[r]^{f_{top\mapsto \mathbb C}} & (\underline\mathcal B GL_{\mathbb C}))^\wedge \\
Sin(\underline\mathcal B T(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell \ar[r]^{f_{top\mapsto \mathbb C}}  \ar[u] & (\underline\mathcal B T_{\mathbb C})^\wedge \ar[u]
}
\end{equation}
(where $T_n(\mathbb C) \hookrightarrow GL_n(\mathbb C)$ is the subgroup of diagonal matrices).
The splitting theorem for (complex, topological) $K$-theory  implies that to  prove the homotopy commutativity
of the left square of (\ref{eqn:sigma-||}) (which is the same as the middle square of (\ref{eqn:T-GL})),
 it suffices to show that the larger
square determined by the upper and middle squares of (\ref{eqn:T-GL}) is commutative.  This is 
implied by the commutativity of outer square of (\ref{eqn:T-GL}).

The commutativity of the right squares of (\ref{eqn:sigma}) and (\ref{eqn:sigma-||}) is implied
by Proposition \ref{prop:Frob}.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- prop-21: body_md -->
\label{prop:F-wedge}
 As in Proposition \ref{prop:hom-fiber}, the (geometric) Frobenius map determines the commutative square of $\mathcal F$-spaces
  \begin{equation}
\label{eqn:Fwedge}
\xymatrix{
(\underline\mathcal B (GL_k,S_k^{2,alg}))^\wedge \ar[r]^{F^\wedge} \ar[d]_{(\tau_k)^\wedge} &  
(\underline\mathcal B (GL_k,S_k^{2,alg}))^\wedge \ar[d]^{(\tau_k)^\wedge} \\
(\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge \ar[r]^{F^\wedge}  & (\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge,
}
\end{equation}
which constitutes a map of  $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $S^2$-fibrations
over the weak equivalence $F^\wedge: (\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge \to (\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge$.

Consequently, the maps
$$ \mathcal J_k^\wedge, \ \circ \mathcal J_k^\wedge \circ F^\wedge: \ (\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge \quad \to \quad \underline\mathcal B G_\ell^o(S^2)
\quad \to \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(S^2)$$
are homotopic.
<!-- end prop-21 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Proposition \ref{prop:hom-fiber}  implies for each $I = (i_1,\ldots,i_n) \in \underline\mathcal N({\bf n})$ that
 \begin{equation}
 \label{eqn:Fwedge-I}
 \xymatrix{
((\underline\mathcal B (GL_k,S_k^{2,alg}))^\wedge)_I \ar[r]^{F^\wedge} \ar[d]_{(\tau_k)^\wedge} &  
((\underline\mathcal B (GL_k,S_k^{2,alg}))^\wedge)_I \ar[d]^{(\tau_k)^\wedge} \\
((\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge)_I \ar[r]^{F^\wedge}  & ((\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge)_I ,
}
\end{equation}
induces a map between homotopy fibers of the vertical maps of (\ref{eqn:Fwedge-I})
which is equivalent to $\times_{i=1}^n p^{n_i}: \prod S^{2n_i}_\ell \to\prod S^{2n_i}_\ell$.  
Since the image of $p$  is a unit in $\mathbb Z/\ell$, each of these squares is a 
fiber homotopy equivalence over the homotopy equivalence
$((\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge)_I \to ((\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge)_I $.  This implies the first assertion.

Thus, (\ref{eqn:Fwedge}) implies that the $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $S^2$ fibrations
$(\tau_k)^\wedge , \ (F^\wedge)^*( (\tau_k)^\wedge)$ are equal in $X_\ell{\text -}fib^o((\underline\mathcal B GL_k)$. 
Hence, the classifying maps
for $(\tau_k)^\wedge$ and $(F^\wedge)^*( (\tau_k)^\wedge)$ are equal in 
$Hom_{Ho((\mathcal F{\text -}spec/\underline\mathcal N)_*)}((\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge, \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(X))$.   
This conclusion is equivalent to the second assertion granted that $ \mathcal J_k^\wedge$
is the classifying map for $(\tau_k)^\wedge$.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- prop-22: body_md -->
\label{prop:ell-spectra}
Let $\underline \mathcal B$ be a pointed-connected special $\mathcal F$-space 
over $\underline\mathcal N$ with the property that $\underline\mathcal B_I$ is $\ell$-good for
every $I \in \underline\mathcal N(\bf n), \ n > 0$.   Then the natural map of $\mathcal F$-spaces
\ $\underline\mathcal B  \  \to \ \underline \mathcal B_\ell$ \ induces a homotopy equivalence of connective $\Omega$-spectra
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:specequiv}
\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty(|| \underline\mathcal B ||^+) \times_{{\bf K(\mathbb Z_\ell,0)}} {\bf K(\mathbb Z,0)} \quad \to \quad || \underline \mathcal B_\ell ||^+,
\end{equation}
where $\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty(|| \underline\mathcal B ||^+)$ is the Bousfield $\ell$-completion of the connective $\Omega$-spectrum $|| \underline\mathcal B ||^+$.
<!-- end prop-22 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Because  $\underline\mathcal B_I$ is $\ell$-good for every $I \in \underline\mathcal N(\bf n), \ n > 0$, $\underline\mathcal B (T_n)$ is $\ell$-complete where
$T_n$ is a finite simplicial set with $|T_n| \simeq S^n$ (where the functor $\underline\mathcal B$ sending pointed finite sets
to pointed simplicial sets is extended to finite simplicial sets by taking diagonals).  Consequently, $||\underline\mathcal B_\ell ||$
is a level-wise $\ell$-completion of $|| \underline\mathcal B ||$.

    As shown in \cite{Bar-Bo}, we may replace the Bousfield localization $\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty((|| \underline\mathcal B ||^+)_{[0]}) $
 of the 0-connected spectrum $(|| \underline\mathcal B ||^+)_{[0]}$
by the level-wise $\mathbb Z/\ell$-completion $(|| \underline\mathcal B_\ell ||^+)_{[0]}$, where $(-)_{[0]}$ sends a connective $\Omega$-spectrum
to its 0-connected cover. Consider the diagram of connective $\Omega$-spectra
\begin{equation}
 \label{eqn:Omega-diag}
 \xymatrix{
( \mathbb Z/\ell_\infty(|| \underline\mathcal B ||^+)_{[0]} \ar[r]^=  \ar[d] & (\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty(|| \underline\mathcal B ||^+)_{[0]} \ar[d] &  \ar[l]_-{\approx} \ar[d] (|| \underline \mathcal B_\ell ||^+)_{[0]} \\
 \mathbb Z/\ell_\infty(|| \underline\mathcal B ||^+) \times_{{\bf K(\mathbb Z_\ell,0)}} {\bf K(\mathbb Z,0)} \ar[d]  \ar[r] & \mathbb Z/\ell_\infty(|| \underline\mathcal B ||^+) \ar[d] & ||\underline \mathcal B_\ell ||^+   \ar[l] \ar[d] \\
 {\bf K(\mathbb Z,0)} \ar[r] & {\bf K(\mathbb Z_\ell,0)} & \ar[l] {\bf K(\mathbb Z,0)}
 }
\end{equation}
whose columns are fiber sequences.  We conclude the homotopy equivalence of (\ref{eqn:specequiv}) by
observing that the map $||\underline \mathcal B_\ell ||^+ \to \mathbb Z/\ell_\infty(|| \underline\mathcal B ||^+)$ factors through \\
$\mathbb Z/\ell_\infty(|| \underline\mathcal B ||^+) \times_{{\bf K(\mathbb Z_\ell,0)}}  {\bf K(\mathbb Z,0)}$.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- thm-8: body_md -->
\label{thm:stable-adams}
Let ${\bf kU}$ denote the connective $\Omega$-spectrum of (topological) complex K-theory.
If $p$ and  $\ell$ are distinct primes, then the maps 
$${\bf J}_\ell, \ {\bf J}_\ell \circ (\psi^p)_\ell:  ({\bf kU})_\ell \times_{{\bf K(\mathbb Z_\ell,0)}} {\bf K(\mathbb Z,0)} 
\quad \to \quad {\bf BG_\ell(S)}$$
are equal in the homotopy category of  connective $\Omega$-spectra.
<!-- end thm-8 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
By Proposition \ref{prop:ell-spectra} applied to $\underline\mathcal B$ equal to $(\underline\mathcal B GL(\mathbb C))^{top}_\ell$, to prove the asserted homotopy 
equivalence of spectra it suffices to prove that the compositions
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:step1}
 (\mathcal J)_\ell  , \  (\mathcal J)_\ell \circ (\psi^p)_\ell: (\underline\mathcal B GL(\mathbb C))^{top}_\ell  \
  \to \ \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(S^2)
\end{equation}
are homotopic.  

We consider the following diagram of pointed--connected special $\mathcal F$-spaces
 \begin{equation}
 \label{eqn:st-ad}
 \xymatrix{
Sin(\underline \mathcal B GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell \ar@/_4pc/[dd]_{\mathcal J_\ell} \ar[d]_{(\psi^p)_\ell} \ar[rr]^{f_{top\mapsto k}} & 
& (\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge \ar@/^4pc/[dd]^{\mathcal J_k} \ar[d]^{F^\wedge} \\
Sin(\underline \mathcal B GL(\mathbb C)^{top})_\ell \ar[d]_{\mathcal J_\ell}  \ar[rr]^{f_{top\mapsto k}} & & (\underline\mathcal B GL_k)^\wedge \ar[d]^{(\mathcal J_k)^\wedge} \\
Sin(\underline \mathcal B G^{top}(\mathbb C^+))_\ell  \ar[rr]_{\approx} & &  \underline\mathcal B G_\ell(S^2).
}
\end{equation}
The top square of (\ref{eqn:st-ad}) homotopy
commutes by Theorem \ref{thm:Sul}; the bottom square homotopy commutes by Proposition \ref{prop:relate-Js};
the far right triangle homotopy commutes by Proposition \ref{prop:F-wedge} and Theorem \ref{thm:X-ell-universal}.
Thus, the homotopy commutativity of the far left triangle is implied by these homotopy commutative squares
and  the fact that the horizontal
maps of (\ref{eqn:st-ad}) are homotopy equivalences.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- thm-9: body_md -->
\label{thm:oriented-stable-adams}
Let ${\bf J}_\ell^o:  ({\bf BU})_\ell \ \to \ (\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_\ell$ denote the map 
of 1-connected $\Omega$-spectra induced by ${\bf J}_\ell$.
If $p$ and $\ell$ are distinct primes, then the following maps 
  $${\bf J}_\ell^o, \ {\bf J}_\ell^o \circ (\psi^p)_\ell:  ({\bf BU})_\ell 
\quad \to \quad (\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_\ell $$
are equal in the homotopy category of connective $\Omega$-spectra.
<!-- end thm-9 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
The fiber sequence $ (\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_\ell \ \to  \ \bf{BG_\ell (S)}_{[0]} \ \to  \ {\bf K(\mathbb Z_\ell^*,1)}$
of (\ref{eqn:GS}) determines the exact sequence
$$Hom_{Ho(c.\Omega-spectra)}(({\bf BU})_\ell,{\bf K(\mathbb Z_\ell^*,0)})  \to Hom_{Ho(c.\Omega-spectra)}(({\bf BU})_\ell,(\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_\ell)$$
$$ \quad \to \quad Hom_{Ho(c.\Omega-spectra)}(({\bf BU})_\ell,\bf{BG_\ell(S)}_{[0]}).$$
Consider the difference  
$${\bf J}_\ell^o - {\bf J}_\ell^o \circ (\psi^p)_\ell \in Hom_{Ho(c.\Omega-spectra)}(({\bf BU})_\ell,(\bf{BG^o(S}_{[0]})_\ell).$$
By Theorem \ref{thm:stable-adams}, this difference maps to 0 in 
$Hom_{Ho(c.\Omega-spectra)}(({\bf BU})_\ell,\bf{BG_\ell(S)}_{[0]})$ and thus is an element of 
$Hom_{Ho(c.\Omega-spectra)}(({\bf BU})_\ell,{\bf K(\mathbb Z_\ell^*,0)}) $.  Because 
 $({\bf BU})_\ell$ is 0-connected, $Hom_{Ho(c.\Omega-spectra)}(({\bf BU})_\ell,{\bf K(\mathbb Z_\ell^*,0)}) = 0$ so that
$${\bf J}_\ell^o \ =  \ {\bf J}_\ell^o \circ (\psi^p)_\ell \ \in \ Hom_{Ho(c.\Omega-spectra)}(({\bf BU})_\ell,(\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_\ell)$$ 
as asserted.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
\label{rem:false}
The maps  ${\bf J}_\ell, \ {\bf J}_\ell \circ (\psi^p)_\ell$ of Theorem \ref{thm:oriented-stable-adams}  
factor through $\bf{BG_\ell^o(S)} \to \bf{BG_\ell(S)}$.  However, as explained in \cite{B-K}, these 
factorizations are not equal in\\
 $Hom_{Ho(c.\Omega-spectra)}(({\bf kU})_\ell \times_{\bf {K(\mathbb Z_\ell,0)}} {\bf K(\mathbb Z,0)},(\bf{BG_\ell^o(S)})$.
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- cor-9: body_md -->
\label{cor:local}
If $p$ and $\ell$ are distinct primes, the following maps of 1-connected spectra
$${\bf J}_{(\ell)}^o, \ {\bf J}_{(\ell)}^o \circ (\psi^p)_{(\ell)}:  ({\bf BU})_{(\ell)} 
\quad \to \quad (\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_{(\ell)} $$
are equal in the homotopy category of  connective $\Omega$-spectra.
<!-- end cor-9 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
We refer to \cite[\S II.13] {JFA} for the $\ell$-localized stable Adams operation, 
$(\psi^p)_{(\ell)}: ({\bf BU})_{(\ell)} \to ({\bf BU})_{(\ell)}$ and observe that the 
$\mathbb Z/\ell$-completions of the maps  ${\bf J}_{(\ell)}^o, \ {\bf J}_{(\ell)}^o \circ (\psi^p)_{(\ell)}$
of $\ell$-local $\Omega$-spectra
are homotopic to the maps of $\mathbb Z/\ell$-complete $\Omega$-spectra ${\bf J}_\ell^o, \ {\bf J}_\ell^o \circ (\psi^p)_\ell.$  
The computations of homotopy groups recalled in Proposition \ref{prop:fingen}
imply that 
\ $(\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_{(\ell)} \ \to \ (\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_\ell $
is a weak equivalence (see \cite[VI.68]{Bo-Kan})), so that the corollary follows from Theorem \ref{thm:oriented-stable-adams}
and the commutativity of the following square of $\Omega$-spectra
\begin{equation}
\label{eqn:com-spectra}
\xymatrix@C6pc{
({\bf BU})_{(\ell)}  \ar[d] \ar[r]^-{{\bf J}_{(\ell)}^o - {\bf J}_{(\ell)}^o \circ (\psi^p)_{(\ell)}} & (\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_{(\ell)} \ar[d] \\
({\bf BU})_\ell \ar[r]_-{{\bf J}_{\ell}^o - \ {\bf J}_{\ell}^o \circ (\psi^p)_{\ell}} & (\bf{BG^o(S)}_{[0]})_\ell .
}
\end{equation}
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
\label{rem:quat}
As in  \cite[Thm 10.5]{F82}, the techniques of this paper apply to prove quaternionic analogs
of the preceding results.  One should replace $GL_n(\mathbb C)$ acting on $\mathbb C^{n+}$ by $Sp_{2n}(\mathbb C)$
acting on $Q^{n+} \simeq S^{4n}$, where $Q^n$ is an $n$-dimensional vector space over the 
division algebra of quaternions (which has dimension 4 over $\mathbb R$).  We leave the details to the reader.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
